Massachusetts Cannabis & Hemp Report — Independent Primary-Source Audit (Part 1)

Last audited: 23 October 2026 Method: Primary sources only — Massachusetts General Laws and session laws on malegislature.gov, Cannabis Control Commission (mass.gov / masscannabiscontrol.com, incl. Open Data Catalog CSVs downloaded and inspected, Municipal Zoning Tracker — all 351 rows, bulletins and releases), Department of Revenue and MDAR on mass.gov, Secretary of the Commonwealth (sec.state.ma.us / official ballot documents), official municipal sites, BIA, govinfo.gov and the Federal Register for federal items. Nothing is confirmed from news, law-firm blogs, trade press or broker listings; where only a secondary source exists the verdict is Unverified. Scope: The complete attached report (Parts 0–12, Runs 1 & 2, plus combined Research Notes). Personal-data rule applied: no personal phone/email for any individual licence holder is reproduced; the CCC licence CSVs publish BUSINESS_EMAIL / BUSINESS_PHONE / EIN_TIN — EIN/TIN must never be reproduced, and individual/sole-proprietor holders are name + official link only. Parts 3–9 were checked and contain no such personal details.

Verdict totals — 319 material claims

Verdict Parts 0–2 Parts 3–6 Parts 7–9 Parts 10–12 Total
Confirmed ~42 71 48 54 ~215
Corrected 18 9 12 11 50
Removed 0 2 0 2 4
Unverified ~18 10 17 5 ~50

Research Notes disposition (33 items, Parts 10–12 audit, Table B): 13 Resolved, 20 Still Unverified — see the consolidated still-unverified list after the tables.

Internal contradictions found and resolved

  1. FY tax revenue "$289M vs $280M vs $336M" — mostly not a contradiction: $289M is FY2025 state-only (sales+excise, CCC Jan 2026 per DOR); ~$336M is FY2026 state+local across three taxes (CCC Sep 2026 per DOR). Different years, different baskets. The only genuine conflict left is $289M vs ~$280M for FY2025 state revenue — Unverified, confirm at DOR.
  2. CCC phone numbers — not a conflict: (833) 869-6820 is the Medical Use Program / mass.gov org line (M–F 10am–3pm); (774) 415-0200 is CCC general inquiries on the CCC's own site. Both official; list both labelled. A third number on DOR's FAQ, (617) 701-8400, appears legacy — do not publish.
  3. ITL count 10 vs 15 — resolved: 10 operating (CCC, 13 Aug 2026). "15 licensed" is trade-press only — Removed.
  4. Waste witness one vs two agents — resolved: current 935 CMR 500.105(12)(d) says ONE agent; two-agent wording is superseded.
  5. Medical reciprocity — CCC's "MA residents only" consumer wording is overbroad; 935 CMR 501.002 controls (non-residents receiving end-of-life/palliative/cancer treatment in MA can qualify).
  6. Medical home grow "hardship-only" vs "12+12" — resolved: 12 flowering + 12 vegetative without hardship (501.140(3)(c)/501.027(8)); hardship registration is only to exceed that. CCC's "hardship programme not yet implemented" page note stays flagged.
  7. Interstate "no trigger law" — wrong as stated: a dormant personal-use trigger exists at c.94G §7(b); there is no commercial trigger/compact.
  8. Hemp window Dec 1 vs Jan 1 — MDAR's official licensing page (Dec 1–Apr 30) governs applicants; the Jan 1 reading comes from a <>-headed copy of 330 CMR 32 — regulation text stays Verify against the Secretary of the Commonwealth print.
  9. Ballot percentages — the report mixed denominators (share of total cast incl. blanks vs share of Yes+No). PD43+ official basis throughout: 2008 Q2 65.2% Yes, 2012 Q3 63.3% Yes, 2016 Q4 53.7% Yes (confirmed).
  10. MTC vs MME in the 280E sections — Ch.65 §§3–4 renamed MTC→MME in c.62/c.63 too; Part 2/Summary updated, ending the internal inconsistency.
  11. Microbusiness fees (CCC vs CCC) — CCC fees page ($0 application / 50% of licence) vs CCC license-types page (50% of combined application+licence): a genuine intra-agency conflict, now disclosed in the text rather than silently resolved.
  12. Delivery waiver count 13 vs live tracker — tracker is authoritative and live: 16 waivers (DW-2026-001…016).

Independent Audit — Massachusetts Cannabis & Hemp, Parts 0–2 ONLY

Report audited: ~/workspace/research_notes/massachusetts-cannabis-hemp-run1-20261024-0124/report.md (RUN 1, Parts 0–6; this audit covers Headlines/Part 0, Part 1 Laws §§1.1–1.9, Part 2 Taxes) Audit date: 2026-10-23 (sources opened 2026-10-23/24 audit pass) Rule applied: PRIMARY SOURCES ONLY — malegislature.gov, mass.gov (CCC/DOR/MDAR/Governor), masscannabiscontrol.com (CCC), sec.state.ma.us (Secretary of the Commonwealth), federalregister.gov, govinfo.gov (enacted Public Laws), congress.gov/CRS via govinfo text. News, law-firm blogs, trade press, secondary summaries were NOT used to confirm. Where only those exist, verdict = Unverified. Verdicts: Confirmed / Corrected / Removed / Unverified

0.2 "§§29–32 amending c.94G §§7 and 13; related thresholds in c.94C §§32L/32M raised 2→3 oz and §34 1 oz→2 oz" (implying §§29–32 do all of this) Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §§7,8,9 (c.94C) vs §§29,30,31,32 (c.94G) "Ch.65 §§29–30 amend c.94G §7 (possession/gifting 1→2 oz); §§31–32 amend c.94G §13 (residence lock threshold 1→2 oz; excess-possession band 1–2 oz → 2–3 oz). Separately, Ch.65 §§7–8 amend c.94C §§32L and 32M (figure '2'→'3' wherever it appears in §32L, and in §32M line 3); §9 amends c.94C §34 ('one ounce'→'2 ounces')." Report conflates the two sets of sections.
0.2 §36 Ownership caps, new c.94G §16: max 6 retailer / 3 FIMTC / 3 manufacturer / 3 cultivator; <20% non-controlling financial interest exempt; ESOP trustees exempt Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section16 (text eff. Apr 19, 2026, verbatim as reported); Ch.65 §36 Add statutory proviso report omits: "a licensee that holds 3 fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center licenses shall not hold any additional medical marijuana establishment licenses established by the commission pursuant to section 7 of chapter 94I." Also note defined term in statute is "fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center licenses" — "FIMTC" is CCC shorthand, not the §16 wording.
0.2 Ownership caps are simply current law from 19 Apr 2026 (no phase-in stated) Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §§60,59 Add: "§60 requires CCC to amend regulations and begin accepting applications under new §16 within 2 months after the effective date, and bars granting a non-social-equity licensee more than 5 retail licences until 12 months after CCC begins accepting such applications. §59 requires a targeted ownership/control audit within 12 months, report within 18 months."
0.2 MTC renamed MME throughout c.94I and regulations Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §§3–5,13,15–17,20,23,34,38–53 (systematic strike/insert "medical marijuana treatment center"→"medical marijuana establishment"; new definition in c.94I §1: "'Medical marijuana establishment', a fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center or any other type of licensed medical use of marijuana-related business") Regulation rename is via CCC emergency regs, not the Act itself — Act amends statutes; do not say Act renames it "throughout the regulations."
0.2 §25 Delivery: limited delivery allowed in any municipality; a municipality that does not authorise retail may request CCC waiver to prohibit delivery, ≤2 years, extendable in 2-year increments Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §25 (new c.94G §4(b)(2), verbatim) Precise wording: waiver "may be extended in 2-year increments upon request, at the commission's discretion" — extension is discretionary, not automatic.
0.2 §21 Advertising: CCC may permit sales/discount/loyalty advertising within an establishment or through opt-in email list only Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §21 (insert after "marijuana" in c.94G §4 line 267: "; provided, however, that the commission may permit advertising, marketing and branding of sales, discounts and customer loyalty programs within a marijuana establishment, or through an opt-in email list") —
0.2 §23 New c.94G §23: 60-day credit limit, delinquent-payment list, $5,000 penalty per violation Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §37 (new §23(b),(g): ≤60 days; fine ≤$5,000 per violation) and §66 Add effective-date correction: "§23 is enacted by Ch.65 §37, but Ch.65 §66 provides 'Section 37 shall take effect on January 1, 2028.' It is NOT in effect in Oct 2026." Report presents it as a current Ch.65 change with no delayed-effect warning — material error.
0.2 Directed studies: use-patterns report by 2028-01-01 (§55); market/licence study published by 2028-07-01 (§56); CCC study of intoxicating hemp (sourced to Legislature press release) Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 §§55,56,57,58,64 §§55/56 Confirmed as stated (§55 submit ≤Jan 1, 2028; §56 publish + submit ≤July 1, 2028). Hemp study is NOT merely a press release: it is Ch.65 §64 — CCC, in consultation with DPH, MDAR, local boards of health, DOR and ABCC, must study effective regulation of the hemp-derived cannabinoid market and publish/submit results not later than December 15, 2026. Report also omits §57 (CCC study of c.64N excise-tax sustainability, report ≤Jan 1, 2028) and §58 (workplace-safety/enforcement review ≤July 1, 2027, report ≤July 1, 2028).

1.3 Possession §7(a)(1): 2 oz or equivalent as determined by commission; §7(a)(2): ≤10 oz at primary residence + ≤6 plants/person, ≤12/premises; §7(a)(4): gift ≤2 oz, no advertising/promotion Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section7 (current text, verbatim) Report's quote drops the pre-amendment 5 g concentrate cap correctly — new (1)/(4) have NO 5 g sub-cap in statute; equivalency is commission-determined. State this explicitly to avoid readers importing the old 5 g cap.

(c) exception is for a designated area of an establishment in a municipality that voted to allow on-premises consumption — report accurate.

1.4 Card process: 18+ MA residents; <18 needs two MA certifying providers (one board-certified paediatrician/subspecialist) + safeguards; 60-day supply; no patient registration fee Corrected (in part) / Unverified (fee) https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ ("Qualifying Patients under 18 must be approved by two Massachusetts-licensed certifying physicians, who must diagnose… a debilitating life-limiting illness or condition"; over-18: certified + "possess up to a 60-day supply") CCC Know the Laws does NOT require one of the two to be a paediatrician/subspecialist in the text opened — that pinpoint (and parental-consent/neurology details, cited to a CCC certifying-provider page not re-opened) = Unverified. 1.5 Federal Delay PL 119-103 §2019 — report flags Verify, citing only secondary sources (Vicente LLP, National Agricultural Law Center) Corrected (flag RESOLVED) https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ103/html/PLAW-119publ103.htm §2019 (PL 119-103, H.R.6500, Sept. 2, 2026: "Until December 11, 2026, the amendments made by section 781 of division B of Public Law 119-37 (7 U.S.C. 1639o note) shall only apply with respect to products described in paragraphs (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I) of section 297A… (7 U.S.C. 1639o) (as amended by such section 781)") REMOVE Verify. Corrected wording: "PL 119-103 (H.R.6500, Continuing Appropriations and Extensions Act, 2027, signed Sept. 2, 2026) §2019, 'Section 781 Extension', provides that until December 11, 2026 the §781 amendments apply only to intermediate/final products described in (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I) — i.e. products containing cannabinoids not capable of being naturally produced by Cannabis sativa L. The broader total-THC standard, synthesised-outside-the-plant exclusion, intermediate 0.3% cap and final 0.4 mg/container cap therefore do not apply until Dec 11, 2026 on the enacted text. §2019 does not move §781's Nov 12, 2026 effective date; it narrows its application until Dec 11." Report's secondary-source summary was substantively accurate, but must now cite GovInfo, not law-firm/national-center sources.

Conflict Resolution — DOR Revenue (Part 2)

  • Resolved (scope): $289M ≠ $336M is NOT a conflict. $289M = FY2025, state only, sales + excise (CCC Jan 2026, citing DOR). ~$336M = FY2026, state + local, sales + local + excise (CCC Sept 2026, citing DOR). Different fiscal year, different tax basket.
  • Unresolved (genuine conflict): FY2025 state figure $289M (CCC Jan 2026 release, precise, citing DOR) vs ~$280M (CCC Feb 2026 release as reported; A&F voter-guide fiscal statement ~$280M state + ~$50M local). This audit opened the $289M primary but not the Feb release or the A&F statement, and no DOR revenue report itself was opened. Verdict: Unverified — do not average or pick one. Where to confirm: (1) DOR FY2025 / June 2025 monthly revenue report and DOR marijuana collections data on mass.gov; (2) CCC Feb 2026 "$9 billion" release on masscannabiscontrol.com; (3) Secretary of Administration & Finance fiscal-consequences statement on the Secretary of the Commonwealth's Question 8 page (sec.state.ma.us). DOR is authoritative; CCC releases are republication.
  • FY2026 $336M and $43M local / $2B cumulative are Confirmed as CCC-published, DOR-attributed figures, not as independently verified DOR data.

4.4 Ownership caps, c.94G §16 (Ch.65): max 6 retailer / 3 FIMTC / 3 product manufacturer / 3 cultivator; <20% financial interest without control exempt; ESOP trustees exempt Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section16 Caps and exemptions are verbatim-correct, BUT the report omits the statutory/regulatory PHASE-IN for the 6th retail licence: under Ch.65 as implemented by the CCC's June 2026 emergency regulations, for the first 12 months after the Commission begins accepting applications under Ch.65, ONLY Social Equity Businesses may be granted a 6th retailer licence and non-SEBs may hold at most 5. Corrected wording: add "Phase-in: during the first 12 months after the CCC begins accepting applications under Ch.65, a 6th retailer licence is available only to Social Equity Businesses; non-SEB licensees are capped at 5 retailer licences in that period; thereafter the cap is 6 for all." Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/07/cannabis-control-commission-seeks-feedback-on-chapter-65-emergency-regulations/ Also add §16's further proviso: a holder of 3 FIMTC licences "shall not hold any additional medical marijuana establishment licenses" under c.94I §7
4.4 Cultivator moratorium to 30 June 2027 — CCC bulletin 9 Oct 2026; first approved 16 Apr 2026, discussed 11 June 2026; covers Indoor/Outdoor Cultivator applications and non-majority-equity Microbusiness applications; does NOT apply to Microbusiness applications majority equity-owned by eligible SEP participants or Economic Empowerment Applicants Confirmed https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/10/bulletin-temporary-moratorium-of-marijuana-cultivator-license-applications-oct-9-2026/ — Bulletin verbatim: vote at 8 Oct 2026 meeting to extend the pause "previously approved… on April 16, 2026 and further discussed… June 11, 2026"; "does not apply to Microbusiness license applications where the applicant is majority equity-owned by eligible Social Equity Program (SEP) Participants or Economic Empowerment Applicants (EEAs), as defined in 935 CMR 500.002"; "This suspension will remain in effect until June 30, 2027. The Commission may shorten or extend…" — add the shorten/extend reservation to the rewrite
4.5 Legal basis: c.94G §22 (SEP) and §14A (Cannabis Social Equity Trust Fund, 15% of Marijuana Regulation Fund revenues) Confirmed https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ Trust Fund funding mechanism confirmed in CCC releases; §22/§14A citations cross-checked in Part 1/2 audit scope
4.5 Ch.65 defines Social Equity Business as ≥51% owned by SEP-eligible individuals or with EE-qualifying ownership Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Ch.65 §14: "not less than 51 per cent majority ownership of individuals who are eligible for the social equity program under section 22 or whose ownership qualifies it as an economic empowerment priority applicant"
4.5 SEP eligibility (Aug 2026 opening): (1) household income ≤400% AMI + residence ≥5 of past 10 years in an ADI; (2) MA resident ≥12 months with c.94C conviction/CWOF or equivalent elsewhere; (3) MA resident ≥12 months who is spouse/child of such a person; (4) original EEA meeting EE criteria (ADI residence, service experience, Black/African American/Hispanic/Latino descent, or other articulable empowerment demonstration) Confirmed https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/08/massachusetts-cannabis-control-commission-opens-social-equity-program-to-new-applicants/ — All four routes verbatim in CCC release

Internal contradictions — resolution

  1. Microbusiness fees (CCC vs CCC, carried into Part 4): Fees page ($0 application / 50% of licence fees) vs License Types page (50% of combined application + licence fees). Report follows the fees page silently. → Corrected at 4.1: state both, flag the conflict.
  2. Ownership cap (Part 0/4.4 vs Ch.65 implementation): Report's flat "6 retailer" cap omits the 12-month SEB-only phase-in for the 6th licence (non-SEB max 5). → Corrected at 4.4.
  3. Delivery waiver count (Part 3.2 vs live Tracker): Secondary "13 waivers" is superseded by the Tracker's 16 (DW-2026-001–016). → Corrected at 3.2.
  4. Social consumption (Part 3.2 Verify vs live Tracker): Full read resolves it — zero opt-ins. → Corrected at 3.2.
  5. Price series (Part 5 vs Part 6): Catalog dataset titled "per Ounce" vs per-gram CSV file used for Part 6 prices; and Nov 2018 $14.09 (Industry Report) vs $14.17 (CSV). Both are real CCC-vs-CCC discrepancies, not report errors. → Corrected labelling at 5.2; series-labelling resolution at 6.4.
  6. Agent counts (Part 6.2): ~21,000 (Jan 2026, adult+medical, to date) vs ~20,000 registrations / 15,000 individuals (Aug/Sep 2026, adult-use only). NOT a contradiction — different scope and date; report's handling is correct. Confirmed.
  7. No other Part 3–6 internal contradictions found. Fee figures in Part 4 are internally consistent and match the CCC fees page exactly in every row checked (all 11 cultivator tiers, both fee columns).

Personal contact details — removal check (name + link only rule)

  • Parts 3–6 of the report list NO individual licence holders (Part 5 is an inventory of data sources only, by design). No personal phone number or email of an individual licence holder is reproduced anywhere in Parts 3–6. Nothing to remove on that ground. Compliant.
  • WARNING for Part 5 / any rewrite: the two CCC licence CSVs themselves publish, for every holder, BUSINESS_EMAIL, BUSINESS_PHONE and EIN_TIN (verified in the downloaded headers, e.g. first data row of l_licenses_commence_ops.csv). The report correctly notes these fields exist without reproducing values. In the rewrite: (a) NEVER reproduce EIN/TIN values; (b) for holders that are individuals / sole proprietors, the "business" email and phone may be personal — reproduce name + official source link only; (c) CCC press-contact details in Part 6 releases (named CCC staff, Press@CCCMass.Com, staff phone numbers) are official agency business contacts, not licence-holder personal data — permissible, but they are not material to Parts 3–6 and need not be carried into the rewrite.
  • No other personal-data items were found in Parts 3–6.

Massachusetts Cannabis & Hemp — Independent Audit, Parts 7–9 ONLY

Report audited: ~/workspace/research_notes/massachusetts-cannabis-hemp-run2-20261024-0139/report.md (Run 2, Parts 7–12; this audit covers Part 7 Hemp Licences, Part 8 Cannabis-Zoned Properties, Part 9 Regulators & History only) Audit date: 2026-10-23 (America/New_York) Method: Primary sources only — MDAR mass.gov hemp pages, 330 CMR 32.00 text as hosted by mass.gov, malegislature.gov statute text (c.128 §§116–123), CCC masscannabiscontrol.com / mass.gov org page, DOR mass.gov, Secretary of the Commonwealth PD43+ (electionstats.state.ma.us), UMass Amherst official poll release. Broker/trade/news sources were NOT used to confirm anything; property listings and non-UMass polls are Unverified by definition. Original report NOT edited.

Headline resolutions

  • CCC phone conflict RESOLVED — neither number is wrong. mass.gov org page lists (833) 869-6820 (M–F 10am–3pm); CCC's own site/releases consistently list (774) 415-0200. Both are current official numbers on different official channels. Present both, labelled by channel.
  • Application-window conflict NOT fully resolvable: MDAR licensing page (current programme page) says December 1 – April 30; 330 CMR 32.03(2)(c) text says January 1 – April 30. The only regulation text readable in this audit is the mass.gov-hosted copy headed <<DRAFT>>; the official PDF linked from the mass.gov regulations page could not be text-read. Keep Verify — confirm against the official print/PDF from the Secretary of the Commonwealth (see Still Unverified).
  • Ballot percentages in the report mix denominators. Vote counts are correct in all three years, but PD43+ official percentages (share of Yes+No) are 2008: 65.2% Yes; 2012: 63.3% Yes; 2016: 53.7% Yes. The report's 2008 "63%" and 2012 "60%" are shares of total votes cast including blanks and must be labelled or replaced.
  • Medical-programme transfer date resolved: 23 December 2018 (CCC contemporaneous release + CCC Guidance for Municipalities). A later CCC slide saying 24 December is wrong.
  • First adult-use sales date upgraded from Verify to Confirmed: 20 November 2018 (CCC official presentation + CCC sales-milestone release).
  • First medical sales date stays Unverified as a sales date: DPH's official RMD status document gives Alternative Therapies Group "Approved to Sell 6/23/15"; the 24 June opening rests on contemporaneous secondary reporting. Do NOT use the CCC slide's "June 25" date.
  • Hemp licensee list Verify resolved: dedicated official page now exists — https://www.mass.gov/info-details/massachusetts-hemp-licensee-list
  • Personal-data check: PASS. Parts 7–9 as written contain NO personal phone/email for any individual hemp licence holder — only programme/agency public contacts. Removal instruction for any rewrite is in row 77.

Massachusetts Cannabis & Hemp — Independent Audit: Parts 10–12 + Research Notes + Federal Items

Audited files: - Run 2: ~/workspace/research_notes/massachusetts-cannabis-hemp-run2-20261024-0139/report.md (Parts 10–12, Research Notes / Could not verify) - Run 1 (federal items only, grep PL 119-37 / PL 119-103 / 91 FR 22714): ~/workspace/research_notes/massachusetts-cannabis-hemp-run1-20261024-0124/report.md

Audit date: 2026-10-23 (sources accessed 2026-10-23) Method: PRIMARY SOURCES ONLY — malegislature.gov statute text, CCC-posted 935 CMR 500 Ch.65 Emergency Regulations and 935 CMR 501 (eff. 2026-01-02) at masscannabiscontrol.com, CCC official releases / Know the Laws page, BIA / Federal Register Indian Entities list, govinfo.gov (Federal Register + Public Laws). No claim was confirmed from news, law-firm blogs, trade press or secondary summaries. Where only a secondary source exists, verdict = Unverified. Note on CCC PDFs: CCC states the PDFs on its site are unofficial convenience copies; official versions are at the State Bookstore (CCC Know the Laws page). They are the regulator's own current posted text and are treated as the primary regulatory source for this audit, with that caveat.

Part 10 No dormant/trigger interstate-commerce law for commercial trade; no interstate agreement found Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section7 ; §12 and §13 above There IS a dormant personal-use trigger — c.94G §7(b) — and it is broader than Run 2's summary: if import/export "is not prohibited by federal law," a person 21+ shall not be penalised for "possessing, using, purchasing, cultivating, processing or manufacturing any amount of marijuana or marijuana products for personal use." Correct wording: "No dormant/trigger statute authorising commercial interstate commerce and no interstate compact/agreement was found in official sources. The only trigger is personal-use c.94G §7(b), which is inoperative while federal law prohibits import/export. Commercial import/export remains barred in practice by §12(f), §13(e)'s federal-law condition, and 935 CMR 500.105(13)(a)13 (routes must remain within the Commonwealth)."

Part 12 Social consumption hours: default 7:00 a.m.–12:00 a.m. unless municipality authorises otherwise; sales cease ≥30 min before latest authorised time Confirmed Same, §500.141(4)(f): "sales and service shall only occur between the hours of 7:00 A.M. and 12:00 A.M., and shall cease no later than 30 minutes prior to the latest time the licensee is [authorised]" unless municipality explicitly authorises otherwise; municipal bylaws control —
Part 12 Social consumption: no sale/service to visibly intoxicated consumer; 21+ entry with ID verification; consumer information card incl. edibles "delayed up to two hours or more" warning; AIT access to COAs may not collect/store/use personal data; SOPs incl. overserving, impaired-consumer transport (reviewed for comment by municipal chief law enforcement officer), emergency response, agents do not consume while at work Confirmed Same, §500.141(2)(n)–(p),(y), (4)(a)2–3, (4)(h), (5), (9)(j) Current text uses ID inspection at entry and point of sale (§500.141(5)(b)–(c)); "ID-scanning" is Run 2's gloss — corrected wording: "age verification by inspection of proof of identification on entry and at point of sale (§500.141(5))."
Part 12 Social consumption prohibitions §500.141(9) as listed in Run 2 Part 12 (no agent infusion, no agent consumption on shift, no outside product, no consumption contests/games or marijuana prizes, no consumption outside designated areas, no advertising prohibited practices) Corrected Same, §500.141(9)(a)–(j) current text Current (9) list is: (a) sell other than as authorised by §500.141; (b) permit sale/consumption in violation of §500.160; (c) agent infusion/combining into food/drink (consumer self-infusion of lawfully purchased product expressly allowed); (d) agent consumption during a work shift; (e) sale/consumption of tobacco or alcohol on premises; (f) consumption of product not purchased from the establishment; (g) organised game/contest involving competitive/cooperative consumption or awarding marijuana as a prize; (h) advertise/market/brand a prohibited practice; (i) consumption outside designated Consumption Areas; (j) sell/serve to an intoxicated Consumer. Remove any older 2019 §500.141(9) items if presented as current — notably a flat prohibition on discounted/promotional pricing and on indoor combustible consumption except outdoors, and the "Outdoor Smoking Waiver" scheme — those are superseded.
Part 12 Indoor smoking: negative air pressure, ≥20 complete air changes/hour, MERV 14+ filtration, odor control, separate 6 ACH system for rest of premises, annual certification by MA Licensed Mechanical Engineer, PPE + smoke-free viewing options, immediate vacate/close on ventilation failure, no smoking consumption for ≥48 hours Confirmed Same, §500.141(10)(d)–(j) — Current text expressly permits an Indoor Smoking Consumption Area meeting these requirements; this supersedes the 2019 indoor-smoking prohibition.
Part 12 Social consumption availability: CCC (Oct 2026) expects Supplemental licence applications "in the coming months"; municipalities must opt in first, update zoning, execute HCAs Confirmed https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/10/cannabis-control-commission-recognizes-10-years-of-adult-use-cannabis-in-massachusetts/ ("The Commission expects to begin accepting applications for the Supplemental License type in the coming months"); municipal opt-in via c.94G §3(b) / CCC Municipal Zoning Tracker https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ — No social-consumption licence was shown as open for applications in official sources as of Oct 2026.
Part 12 Social consumption exclusivity: 36-month SEP/EE exclusivity (Run 1 figure, "not re-read" in Run 2) Confirmed CCC 935 CMR 500 §500.050(6)(g) Resolved: "Social Consumption Establishment licenses shall be limited on an exclusive basis to businesses controlled by and with majority ownership comprised of Economic Empowerment Priority Applicants or Social Equity Program Participants; or Microbusinesses and Craft Marijuana Cooperatives, for a period of 36 months beginning on the date on which at least one licensee in each Social Consumption Establishment license class (Supplemental, Hospitality, Event Organizer) has received a notice to commence operations," extendable by Commission vote (+24 months, then further only on affirmative findings). The clock has not started until that commence-operations condition is met.
Part 12 Superseded 2019 §500.141 flat 20 mg Δ9-THC/day on-site cap — "do not use" Confirmed Current §500.141(3) (above) contains no such cap; limits are tied to §7/§500.140 as in the point-of-service row Correctly flagged in Run 2. Ensure the 20 mg figure appears nowhere as current law.
Part 12 Visitors: c.94G §7 has no residency requirement — any person 21+ may purchase up to 2 oz; retailers verify 21+ with government photo ID; daily limit §500.140(3)(a) Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section7 (§7(a)(1) as amended by 2026 c.65 §29: "2 ounces or less… or the equivalent amount… as determined by the commission"; no residency language); https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ ("Out-of-state visitors can purchase marijuana… 21+… valid government-issued ID… must enjoy your purchase in Massachusetts") — Visitors buy adult-use on the same terms as residents.
Part 12 Medical reciprocity: CCC Know the Laws says out-of-state residents cannot purchase medical; only MA-resident Registered Qualifying Patients/caregivers may purchase Corrected https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-501-000-medical-use-of-marijuana-effective-01-02-26-pdf/ §501.002 (Qualifying Patient); https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ Resolved against the regulation: 935 CMR 501.002 defines Qualifying Patient as "(a) a Massachusetts Resident or a non-Massachusetts Resident receiving end-of-life or palliative care or cancer treatment in Massachusetts as determined by a Certifying Healthcare Provider, who is 18+…" (and a parallel under-18 clause). Corrected wording: "Massachusetts does not recognise other states' medical cards (no general reciprocity). The sole non-resident route is registration as a Qualifying Patient under 935 CMR 501.002 if the non-resident is receiving end-of-life or palliative care or cancer treatment in Massachusetts, as determined by a Certifying Healthcare Provider. The CCC Know the Laws blanket statement that only MA residents may purchase is overbroad and should not be quoted without this exception." The regulation/statutory scheme controls over the consumer summary page.
Part 12 Medical home grow: CCC page says patients may grow up to 12 flowering + 12 vegetative plants; Run 1 framed this as conflicting with c.94I §2(d) hardship-only Corrected https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94I/Section2 (§2(d)); 935 CMR 501 §§501.027, 501.140(3)(c) (2026-01-02 text); https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ Resolved — both texts exist and must be read together: (1) 935 CMR 501.140(3)(c): "A Registered Qualifying Patient may possess up to 12 flowering plants and up to 12 Vegetative plants, excluding Clones and cuttings. If one or more Qualifying Patients collectively require more than this amount at one residence in order to maintain a 60-day supply, then a Hardship Cultivation Registration is required." (2) 935 CMR 501.027(8) mirrors this: a patient is "prohibited from possessing or cultivating more than 12 Flowering plants and 12 Vegetative plants… without a Hardship Cultivation Registration." (3) Hardship Cultivation (§501.027(1)–(7); c.94I §2(d)) — financial hardship / physical incapacity / no MME within reasonable distance (reg adds lack of access to essential medicine) — allows a limited number sufficient for a 60-day supply, enclosed/locked only. Corrected wording: do NOT describe medical home grow as "hardship-only." State the 12+12 regulation baseline, with hardship registration required to exceed it / where collective 60-day need requires more. Remaining tension (keep flagged): CCC Know the Laws states "The Commission has not yet implemented the Hardship Cultivation Registration program" — if hardship registration is not implemented, how a patient lawfully exceeds 12+12 is Unverified in practice; confirm with CCC Medical Program.
Part 12 Landlord/tenant: c.94G §2(d)(1) — owner may prohibit/regulate consumption etc. on their property, except a lease shall not prohibit a tenant consuming by means other than smoking unless that would cause the landlord to violate federal law/regulation Confirmed https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section2 — Government landlords' separate power is §2(d)(2) as Run 2 stated.
Part 12 Schools: c.94G §2(d)(3) does not authorise possession/consumption on K-12 school grounds, school buses, youth centres, correctional/detox facilities Confirmed Same, §2(d)(3) —

Confirm via DESE / DPH school-health guidance before stating none exists.

Part 12 Pets/veterinary: FDA has not approved cannabis for any use in animals Confirmed https://www.fda.gov/news-events/public-health-focus/fda-regulation-cannabis-and-cannabis-derived-products-including-cannabidiol-cbd (FDA official Q&A, as quoted in Run 2) — No MA-specific veterinary cannabis/CBD rule was found in official MA sources searched — keep that point Unverified (MA Board of Registration in Veterinary Medicine not reviewed). Federal (Run 1+2) 91 FR 22714: final rule/order effective 28 Apr 2026 placing FDA-approved marijuana drug products AND marijuana/extracts/Δ9-THC subject to a state medical marijuana licence in Schedule III; new 21 CFR 1308.13(g); permit-only import/export; expedited DEA registration for state medical licensees (21 CFR Part 1301) Confirmed https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-04-28/html/2026-08176.htm (91 FR 22714–22723, FR Doc. 2026-08176, AG Order No. 6754-2026; "DATES: Effective April 28, 2026"; Summary: applies "to the extent that any of these are included in an FDA-approved drug product or are subject to a state-issued license to manufacture, distribute, and/or dispense marijuana… for medical purposes") ; Federal Register page https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/04/28/2026-08176/schedules-of-controlled-substances-rescheduling-of-food-and-drug-administration-approved-products Title as published is narrower on its face ("Rescheduling of FDA Approved Products…") — the state-medical-licence category is in the Summary/regulatory text, as Run 1/Run 2 describe. Keep both categories explicit.
Federal Adult-use / non-medical marijuana remains Schedule I after 91 FR 22714; broader 2024 NPRM (89 FR 44597) is not this final rule Confirmed Same govinfo text: rule reaches only FDA-approved products and state-medical-licence marijuana; footnote/preamble framework leaves other marijuana in Schedule I. The FR text itself (fn.11) confirms the PL 119-37 hemp redefinition date (see below) Status of the separate broader rescheduling proceeding / any 2026 hearing or pause was NOT established from a primary Federal Register / DOJ source in this audit — any claim about a hearing 29 June–15 July 2026 or a pause is Unverified here and must not be presented as confirmed.
Federal PL 119-37 §781: amends 7 USC §1639o hemp definition to total THC (incl. THCA) ≤0.3% dry weight; exclusions incl. non-naturally-producible cannabinoids, synthesised/manufactured-outside-plant cannabinoids, intermediate products >0.3%, final products >0.4 mg combined total per container; effective 365 days after enactment (enacted 12 Nov 2025 → 12 Nov 2026) Confirmed https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-04-28/html/2026-08176.htm fn.11 ("As of November 12, 2026, this definition is revised to refer to a 'total tetrahydrocannabinols concentration (including tetrahydrocannabinolic acid) of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis'… Public Law 119-37, sec. 781"); CRS (congress.gov) LSB11381 / IF13136 summary of §781 as cited in Run 1 CRS is a congressional research product, not the statute text itself; the 0.4 mg/container and exclusion details rest on CRS + the FR cross-reference in this audit. For republication, cite the enrolled PL 119-37 text at govinfo.gov (PLAW-119publ37) alongside CRS.
Federal PL 119-103 §2019 delays PL 119-37 §781 to 11 Dec 2026 (Run 1/Run 2: Verify — no official text opened) Corrected https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ103/html/PLAW-119publ103.htm — PL 119-103 (H.R.6500, Continuing Appropriations and Extensions Act, 2027), §2019 "SECTION 781 EXTENSION": "Until December 11, 2026, the amendments made by section 781 of division B of Public Law 119-37 (7 U.S.C. 1639o note) shall only apply with respect to products described in paragraphs (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I) of section 297A of the Agricultural Marketing Act of 1946 (7 U.S.C. 1639o) (as amended by such section 781)." Enacted 2 Sept 2026 (140 Stat. 987) Resolved, with corrected mechanics: §2019 does NOT move §781's effective date and does NOT use the words hemp/cannabis. Until 11 Dec 2026 it limits §781's application to only the two cited paragraphs — (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I), i.e. intermediate and final products containing cannabinoids not capable of being naturally produced by Cannabis sativa L. Those exclusions therefore still apply from 12 Nov 2026. All other §781 amendments (total-THC standard, synthesised-outside-plant exclusion in (II), 0.4 mg/container cap in (iv)(III), etc.) are in practical effect held back until 11 Dec 2026. Corrected wording: "PL 119-103 §2019 limits, until December 11, 2026, the application of PL 119-37 §781 to products described in 7 USC §1639o(1)(C)(ii)(I) and (iv)(I) only (non-naturally-producible cannabinoids)." Do not write simply "§781 is delayed to Dec 11" without this limitation, and do not resolve by assertion what the omission of (II)/(III) means for a specific product — that is a statutory-construction question.
Federal PL 119-103 is H.R.6500, signed 2 Sept 2026 as PL 119-103 Confirmed Same govinfo PLAW-119publ103 text ("Sept. 2, 2026 — [H.R. 6500]"; 140 Stat. 987) — Removes Run 1's Verify on enactment/identity.
Federal DOR marijuana FAQ still describes marijuana as federally Schedule I (Run 1 conflict flag) Confirmed (as an outdated-source flag) 91 FR 22714 (above) supersedes that description for the FDA-approved / state-medical categories; DOR FAQ page itself not re-opened in this audit Keep flag: DOR FAQ wording, if it still says Schedule I without qualification, is outdated as to state-licensed medical marijuana after 28 Apr 2026. Adult-use remains Schedule I.

Part 2 — Corrected Final Report

The complete report, Parts 0–12, with every correction applied in place.

Massachusetts Cannabis & Hemp — Deep Research Report (Corrected Final Report)

Last audited: 23 October 2026

Audit method: Primary sources only — malegislature.gov (statutes, session laws and bill pages), mass.gov (Cannabis Control Commission / CCC, Department of Revenue / DOR, Massachusetts Department of Agricultural Resources / MDAR), sec.state.ma.us (Secretary of the Commonwealth), and govinfo.gov / Federal Register for federal items. No claim was confirmed from news, law-firm blogs, trade press or AI summaries; items supportable only from secondary sources are marked Unverified.

What the audit changed

Major corrections applied in place throughout the report (including Summary, Headlines and Research Notes); no outdated value remains:

  • Chapter 65 citation split: Ch.65 §§7–9 amend c.94C §§32L/32M/§34; §§29–32 amend c.94G §§7/§13 — the two sets are no longer conflated.
  • Credit regime delayed: New c.94G §23 (enacted by Ch.65 §37) is not effective until 1 January 2028 (Ch.65 §66).
  • Ownership caps phased in: Non-equity licensees are capped at 5 retail licences for the first 12 months; the §16 proviso barring a holder of 3 fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center licences from additional medical marijuana establishment licences is stated; non-FIMTC MME licences under Ch.65 §61 are social-equity-exclusive for 24 months.
  • Directed studies completed: Ch.65 §64 hemp study due 15 December 2026, plus the §57 and §58 studies, alongside §§55–56.
  • Delivery waivers corrected to 16 (DW-2026-001–016) with the current town list; social-consumption zoning = zero municipalities with zoning in place (CCC tracker, all 351 rows); the HCA-extension "prior votes rescinded" detail was removed; the §3(a)(2) voter-approval rule was completed (all towns after 31 December 2019 plus the clause (iii) MME floor); Cambridge's 300-ft buffer was added.
  • Fees and equity: The Microbusiness fee conflict between the CCC fees page and licence-types page is disclosed; Social Equity Program figures were updated to CCC September 2026 (nearly 1,300 participants, 100+ equity businesses, ~$60M) with the 1 September–30 November 2026 application window added, and the superseded figures removed.
  • Prices and revenue reframed: The price dataset is correctly titled "Average Monthly Price per Ounce" (distinct from the per-gram CSV); FY revenue figures are separated by basket — $289M FY2025 is state-only, ~$336M FY2026 is state+local — and only the $289M vs ~$280M FY2025 state discrepancy remains Unverified (confirm at DOR).
  • Pardons, ballot and history: The pardon exclusion is persons under 21 at the time of the pardon; Question 8 is cited to durable Secretary of the Commonwealth URLs with the under-21 civil-infraction detail added and its home-grow effect left Unverified; ballot percentages are on a PD43+ (Yes+No) basis everywhere (2008: 65.2/34.8; 2012: 63.3/36.7; 2016: 53.7 confirmed); medical programme transfer on 23 December 2018 and first adult-use sales on 20 November 2018 are Confirmed.
  • Tax and funds: c.94I §8 medical tax exemption is Confirmed verbatim; the §280E discussion uses MME (not MTC) per Ch.65 §§3–4; the Cannabis Social Equity Trust Fund transfer is 15% of prior-fiscal-year deposits into the Marijuana Regulation Fund, administered by the Executive Office of Economic Development per c.94G §14A as amended by Ch.65 §33.
  • Bills re-checked: S.2800 is in Senate Ways & Means; H.168 was folded into the Ch.65 process; H.173/H.143/H.179 died/folded on 29 May 2025; H.117 is in House Ways & Means; S.2663 is in Senate Ways & Means.
  • Hemp corrected: 330 CMR 32.03(1) provides only a License for Production and a License for Research — Producer/Processor/Dual are MDAR programme types; Research applicants do need an FBI Identity History Summary; c.128 §118(f) confidentiality is narrowed to facility address, security schematics and GPS; the MDAR licensee list URL is https://www.mass.gov/info-details/massachusetts-hemp-licensee-list ; the application window is December 1–April 30 per the MDAR page (the January 1 regulation-text reading stays Verify); the ABCC hemp-enforcement role (30 May 2024 Advisory) was added.
  • Property listings: Every Part 8 listing is labelled Unverified (broker-sourced, zoning unstated in every case).
  • Contacts: Both CCC phone numbers are listed and labelled — (833) 869-6820 (Medical Program, mass.gov organisation page, Monday–Friday 10am–3pm) and (774) 415-0200 (CCC general) — and DOR's legacy number is not published. No personal phone number or email for an individual licence holder, and no EIN/TIN, appears anywhere.
  • Operations resolved: Interstate commerce — a dormant personal-use trigger exists at c.94G §7(b) (no commercial trigger or compact found); Independent Testing Laboratories = 10 operating (CCC, 13 August 2026); waste witnessing is by one agent under §500.105(12)(d); vendor samples are at §500.140(12); the §500.141(9) prohibitions list is current; social-consumption identification is inspection of proof of ID; social-consumption exclusivity is 36 months under §500.050(6)(g) with its start trigger and 24-month extension.
  • Medical rules resolved: Reciprocity is resolved per 935 CMR 501.002 (no general reciprocity; non-residents receiving end-of-life, palliative or cancer treatment in Massachusetts can qualify); medical home grow is resolved (12 flowering + 12 vegetative plants without hardship registration; hardship registration is only to exceed that, and the CCC page stating the hardship programme is not yet implemented is flagged).
  • Federal hemp delay resolved: PL 119-103 §2019 is quoted verbatim from govinfo — it limits application of PL 119-37 §781 until 11 December 2026 and does not move the effective date.
  • Tribal finding is worded "none found in official sources searched".

Date accessed for all sources: 2026-10-23. Every fact below carries its source link and that access date. Facts are drawn from official sources opened directly (Massachusetts General Laws at malegislature.gov, Cannabis Control Commission [CCC], Mass.gov/DOR/MDAR, Congress.gov/CRS, Federal Register) wherever possible. Anything uncertain, conflicting, or not found on an official source is flagged Verify or Unavailable in line. Proposed bills and the November 2026 ballot question are not current law and are labelled as such.

Summary

Massachusetts has both legal adult-use (21+) and legal medical cannabis under state law. The single biggest development is Chapter 65 of the Acts of 2026 ("An Act Modernizing the Commonwealth's Cannabis Laws," H.5350, signed 19 April 2026), which: restructured the CCC to 3 governor-appointed commissioners; doubled lawful possession/purchase from 1 oz to 2 oz; imposed new per-licensee ownership caps (6 retailer / 3 fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center [FIMTC] / 3 product manufacturer / 3 cultivator licences, with a phase-in under Ch.65 §§59–60 barring a non-social-equity licensee from more than 5 retail licences until 12 months after the CCC begins accepting applications under new c.94G §16); renamed Medical Marijuana Treatment Centers (MTCs) as Medical Marijuana Establishments (MMEs); permitted adult-use delivery statewide subject to municipal waivers; and allowed limited in-store/opt-in-email discount advertising. Adult-use tax remains 10.75% excise + 6.25% state sales tax + up to 3% local option (max 20% combined); medical sales are tax-exempt; Massachusetts decoupled from IRC §280E for licensed businesses from tax year 2022 (St. 2022, c.180). Adult-use gross sales exceeded $1.65B in CY2025 and crossed $10B cumulative on 5 September 2026 (CCC). A temporary CCC moratorium on new cultivator licences runs to 30 June 2027. Hemp is regulated by MDAR under MGL c.128 §§116–123 and 330 CMR 32.00; intoxicating hemp-derived products (incl. delta-8 and CBD food) are effectively barred from general retail under current MDAR rules; the new federal hemp definition (PL 119-37 §781) takes effect 12 November 2026, with PL 119-103 §2019 narrowing its application until 11 December 2026 to intermediate/final products containing cannabinoids not capable of being naturally produced (enacted text: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ103/html/PLAW-119publ103.htm — Accessed 2026-10-23). A ballot question (Question 8) on 3 November 2026 would, if approved, repeal adult-use retail legalisation effective 1 January 2028 — it is a proposal, not current law.

Part 0 — Headlines

  1. Both adult-use and medical cannabis are legal under Massachusetts state law. Adult use: voter Question 4 (2016), first retail sales November 2018 (Northampton and Leicester). Medical: 2012 ballot law; programme transferred from the Department of Public Health to the CCC on 23 December 2018 (Confirmed). Source: CCC contemporaneous release, https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2018/12/administration-of-massachusetts-medical-marijuana-program-to-transfer-to-cannabis-control-commission/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  2. Chapter 65 of the Acts of 2026 (H.5350) — the major 2026 reform. Bill history: conference report accepted by House 155–0 on 2026-04-08 and by Senate 2026-04-09; enacted 2026-04-09 (Senate enacted Yeas 33–Nays 6); "Signed by the Governor, Chapter 65 of the Acts of 2026" on 4/19/2026; an emergency law. Sources: https://malegislature.gov/Bills/194/H5350 and full text https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - §1 creates new MGL c.6 §223: the CCC now has 3 commissioners appointed by the governor (previously 5); the chair is co-terminus with the governor, other commissioners serve 4 years; at least one commissioner must have a social justice background; quorum is 2. Ch.65 §62 provides that the terms of all sitting commissioners terminate on the effective date, with new appointments within 30 days after. - Possession/purchase doubled from 1 oz to 2 oz, effective 2026-04-19. Ch.65 §§29–30 amend c.94G §7 (possession/gifting 1→2 oz); §§31–32 amend c.94G §13 (residence lock threshold 1→2 oz; excess-possession band 1–2 oz → 2–3 oz). Separately, Ch.65 §§7–8 amend c.94C §§32L and 32M (figure "2"→"3" wherever it appears in §32L, and in §32M line 3); §9 amends c.94C §34 ("one ounce"→"2 ounces"). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - New ownership caps (§36, new c.94G §16): "No licensee shall be granted more than 6 marijuana retailer licenses, 3 fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center licenses, 3 marijuana product manufacturer licenses or 3 marijuana cultivator licenses." A financial interest below 20% without control is exempt; ESOP trustees are exempt. The statute provides: "a licensee that holds 3 fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center licenses shall not hold any additional medical marijuana establishment licenses established by the commission pursuant to section 7 of chapter 94I." The defined term in the statute is "fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center licenses" — "FIMTC" is CCC shorthand, not the §16 wording. Phase-in: Ch.65 §60 requires the CCC to amend regulations and begin accepting applications under new §16 within 2 months after the effective date, and bars granting a non-social-equity licensee more than 5 retail licences until 12 months after the CCC begins accepting such applications; §59 requires a targeted ownership/control audit within 12 months, with a report within 18 months. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - "Medical Marijuana Treatment Center (MTC)" is renamed "Medical Marijuana Establishment (MME)" throughout c.94I by Ch.65 §§3–5, 13, 15–17, 20, 23, 34, 38–53, with a new definition in c.94I §1: "'Medical marijuana establishment', a fully integrated medical marijuana treatment center or any other type of licensed medical use of marijuana-related business." The rename in the regulations is via CCC emergency regulations, not the Act itself. - Delivery: limited delivery is allowed in any municipality; a municipality that does not authorise retail may request a CCC waiver to prohibit delivery, valid up to 2 years; the waiver "may be extended in 2-year increments upon request, at the commission's discretion" — extension is discretionary, not automatic (§25; c.94G §4(b)(2) as amended). - Advertising: the CCC may permit advertising of sales/discounts/loyalty programmes within an establishment or through an opt-in email list only (§21). - New c.94G §23: 60-day credit limit between licensees, a delinquent-payment list, and a $5,000 penalty per violation — enacted by Ch.65 §37 but NOT in effect in October 2026: Ch.65 §66 provides "Section 37 shall take effect on January 1, 2028." Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - Directed studies: cannabis use patterns report, to be submitted not later than January 1, 2028 (§55); market/licence-number study, to be published and submitted not later than July 1, 2028 (§56); CCC study of c.64N excise-tax sustainability, report not later than January 1, 2028 (§57); workplace-safety/enforcement review by July 1, 2027, with a report not later than July 1, 2028 (§58); and, under §64, the CCC, in consultation with DPH, MDAR, local boards of health, DOR and ABCC, must study effective regulation of the hemp-derived cannabinoid market and publish/submit results not later than December 15, 2026 — a study direction, not a hemp sales regime. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  3. Host Community Agreement (HCA) reform (2022–2024, now codified). St. 2022, c.180 ("An Act Relative to Equity in the Cannabis Industry") gave the CCC authority to review and approve HCAs and set equity standards for municipalities; the rules are now in 935 CMR 500.180 (HCA Requirements) and 500.181 (Minimum Acceptable Equity Standards Governing Municipalities) — regulation numbers Verify (confirm in the CCC-posted regulation PDFs linked from https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/) — and in c.94G §3(d): the community impact fee must be reasonably related to documented municipal costs, not more than 3% of gross sales, not effective after the eighth year of operation, and the HCA may impose no additional monetary, in-kind, or charitable obligations. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23. In October 2026 the CCC voted (Oct 8, 2026) to continue administrative licence extensions for licensees needing more time to submit a compliant HCA or HCA Waiver, with new extensions requiring DOR good standing — Verify (CCC bulletin not opened directly; reported via republication of the bulletin).
  4. Blanket marijuana pardon. The Governor's pardon "forgives all Massachusetts state court misdemeanour convictions for simple possession of marijuana before March 13, 2024" and took effect April 3, 2024 when the Governor's Council approved it. It excludes distribution/intent, OUI, out-of-state/federal convictions, CWOFs and non-convictions, criminal cases resolved after March 13, 2024, and persons who are under the age of 21 at the time of the pardon. It does not automatically seal or expunge a record; a certificate form (with a sealing option) or a separate seal/expunge request is required, and there are no fee/fine refunds. Source (official): https://www.mass.gov/info-details/marijuana-pardons-frequently-asked-questions — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  5. Federal rescheduling — medical only. A final federal order effective April 28, 2026 (91 Fed. Reg. 22714) placed (i) FDA-approved drug products containing marijuana and (ii) marijuana, extracts and delta-9 THC subject to a state-issued medical marijuana licence in Schedule III, with permit-only import/export and an expedited DEA registration route (21 CFR Part 1301) for state medical licensees. Source (official): https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/04/28/2026-08176/schedules-of-controlled-substances-rescheduling-of-food-and-drug-administration-approved-products — Accessed 2026-10-23. Adult-use/non-medical marijuana remains Schedule I; the broader rescheduling proceeding was reported paused in Sept–Oct 2026 secondary sources — Verify. Note: the DOR marijuana tax FAQ still describes marijuana as federally Schedule I — outdated; see Part 2.
  6. Market scale (CCC official). Adult-use gross sales were over $1.65 billion in CY2025 (a record), with 46.3 million transactions; cumulative adult-use gross sales crossed $10 billion on September 5, 2026 ($10,008,567,111 at announcement), with 782 operating businesses and 91 medical dispensaries as of September 2026. Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ and https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  7. Cultivation moratorium. By CCC bulletin of October 9, 2026, the Commission extended its temporary pause on accepting new Indoor/Outdoor Marijuana Cultivator licence applications (first approved April 16, 2026; discussed June 11, 2026) until June 30, 2027; the moratorium also covers Marijuana Microbusiness applications that are not majority equity-owned, and expressly does not apply to Microbusiness applications majority equity-owned by eligible Social Equity Program participants or Economic Empowerment Applicants. The CCC states it "may shorten or extend the suspension if it determines that market conditions warrant." Source (official CCC bulletin): https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/10/bulletin-temporary-moratorium-of-marijuana-cultivator-license-applications-oct-9-2026/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  8. Question 8 (ballot proposal — NOT current law). The official Secretary of the Commonwealth sample ballot summary states the proposed law would repeal the laws legalising, regulating and taxing retail adult-use marijuana; allow 21+ possession of up to 1 oz (including up to 5 g concentrate) and gifting of the same; impose a $100 civil penalty plus forfeiture for possession of more than 1 oz up to 2 oz; provide that, for persons 21 and younger [sic in the Attorney General summary; i.e. under-21], possession of up to 2 oz would be a civil infraction: $100 fine, forfeiture, drug awareness programme, community service, and parental/guardian notification; provide for expedited conversion of adult-use licensees to medical dispensaries; retain the CCC for the medical programme only; and, if approved on November 3, 2026, take effect January 1, 2028. Source: Secretary of the Commonwealth, https://www.sec.state.ma.us/divisions/elections/research-and-statistics/2026-Information-for-voters/2026-ballot-questions.htm (election date confirmed at https://www.sec.state.ma.us/divisions/elections/research-and-statistics/information-for-voters-2026.htm) — Accessed 2026-10-23. Whether home cultivation would survive is Unverified: the Attorney General summary is silent on home cultivation and the full proposed-law text (linked from the Secretary's Question 8 page) was not opened line-by-line — do not state either outcome from the summary; confirm in the full text on the Secretary's Question 8 page.

Part 1 — Laws

1.1 Legal status

  • Adult-use (recreational): legal for adults 21+ under MGL c.94G. Medical: legal under MGL c.94I, administered by the CCC since 23 December 2018 (Confirmed; CCC contemporaneous release, https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2018/12/administration-of-massachusetts-medical-marijuana-program-to-transfer-to-cannabis-control-commission/). Both remain illegal under federal law except marijuana in FDA-approved products / under a state medical licence, moved to Schedule III by the April 2026 order (Part 0, item 5); other marijuana remains federally illegal (Schedule I). Sources: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94I ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

1.2 Statute and regulation index (official text)

MGL c.94G — Regulation of the Use and Distribution of Marijuana Not Medically Prescribed. Chapter index: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G — Accessed 2026-10-23. Sections: §1 Definitions; §2 Limitations on the chapter; §3 Local control; §4 Cannabis Control Commission; §5 Licensing of marijuana establishments; §6 Expiration and renewal of licences; §7 Personal use of marijuana; §8 Marijuana accessories; §9 Lawful operations; §10 Contracts; §11 Professional services; §12 General operational requirements; §13 Penalties; §14 Marijuana Regulation Fund; §14A Cannabis Social Equity Trust Fund; §15 Independent testing laboratories; §16 License limits (new, Ch.65 of 2026); §17 Research agenda; §18 Annual audits and reports; §19 Liability; §20 Application of tax chapters; §21 Criminal history checks / suitability; §22 Social equity program; §23 Credit limits between licensees (new, Ch.65 of 2026 — enacted by Ch.65 §37, effective January 1, 2028 under Ch.65 §66; not yet in effect in October 2026).

MGL c.94I — Medical Use of Marijuana. Chapter index: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94I — Accessed 2026-10-23. Sections: §1 Definitions; §2 Medical marijuana programme (operation, immunity, cultivation registrations); §3 Database/confidentiality; §4 Healthcare professional registration; §5 Patient registration; §6 Limitations; §7 Rules and fees; §8 Taxation — verbatim: "Marijuana sold pursuant to this chapter shall not be taxed under chapter 64H, 64I, 64K or 64N." Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94I/Section8 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

MGL c.64N — Marijuana Tax (excise and local option): §§2 and 3 quoted in Part 2. MGL c.64H §2 imposes the 6.25% state sales tax referenced by c.64N §2. MGL c.94C (Controlled Substances Act) remains relevant: Ch.65 §§7–8 amend §§32L and 32M (in §32L the figure "2"→"3" in lines 2, 23, 25, 30, 35, 36 and 45; in §32M in line 3 only) and §9 amends §34 ("one ounce"→"2 ounces"); under-21 offences are handled through c.94C §32M drug-awareness provisions (see c.94G §13(f) below). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Hemp statute: MGL c.128 §§116–123. Definition at §116 (quoted verbatim in §1.5 below): https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXIX/Chapter128/Section116 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Regulations — current status (per CCC "Know the Laws" page, opened): - 935 CMR 500.000 — Adult Use of Marijuana: current. The version on the CCC website is the Ch.65 Emergency Regulations, effective June 18, 2026 — Verify (effective date and the §500.001 purpose quote below: confirm by opening the CCC-posted PDF linked from Know the Laws): https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. §500.001 states the purpose: "to implement M.G.L. c. 6, § 223, M.G.L. c. 94G, M.G.L. c. 94I, St. 2012, c. 369, St. 2016, c. 334, St. 2017, c. 55, St. 2022, c. 180, and St. 2026, c. 65." Contents include 500.002 Definitions; 500.003 Colocated Marijuana Operations; 500.005 Fees; 500.050 Marijuana Establishments; 500.100–104 Licensing; 500.105 General Operational Requirements; 500.110 Security Requirements; 500.120 Cultivators; 500.130 Product Manufacturers; 500.140 Retail Sale; 500.141 Social Consumption; 500.145 Delivery; 500.146 Delivery Operators; 500.147 Research; 500.150 Edibles; 500.160 Testing; 500.170 Municipal Requirements; 500.180 HCA Requirements; 500.181 Minimum Acceptable Equity Standards; 500.200 Dukes and Nantucket Counties; enforcement at 500.300+. - 935 CMR 501.000 — Medical Use of Marijuana: current.
