1.3 Personal use: possession, home grow, public use, products

Possession and home grow — MGL c.94G §7 (current text, as amended by St. 2026, c.65 §29, effective April 19, 2026). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section7 — Accessed 2026-10-23. Verbatim operative provisions — it is not a criminal or civil offence for a person 21+ to engage in:

"(1) possessing, using, purchasing, processing or manufacturing 2 ounces or less of marijuana or the equivalent amount of marijuana products as determined by the commission" [prior text was 1 oz] "(2) within the person's primary residence, possessing up to 10 ounces of marijuana and any marijuana produced by marijuana plants cultivated on the premises and possessing, cultivating or processing not more than 6 marijuana plants for personal use so long as not more than 12 plants are cultivated on the premises at once" "(4) giving away… up to 2 ounces… to a person 21 years of age or older; provided… such transfer shall not be advertised or otherwise promoted to the public."

The amended §7(a)(1) and (a)(4) contain no 5 g concentrate sub-cap in the statute — equivalency is as determined by the commission. §7 also protects: parental/caregiver status (§7(d) — a parent or legal guardian shall not be denied custody/visitation solely for lawful conduct under the chapter); organ-transplant eligibility (§7(e)); and, for 21+ persons, possessing, producing, processing, manufacturing, purchasing, obtaining, selling, transferring or delivering hemp (§7(f)).

Penalties — MGL c.94G §13 (current text). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section13 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - (a) Home cultivation visible from a public place, or not in a locked area: civil penalty up to $300 + forfeiture. - (b) (as amended 2026) Possessing more than 2 ounces in a residence unless kept locked: civil penalty up to $100 + forfeiture. - (c) Public consumption, or smoking where tobacco smoking is prohibited: up to $100; exception for a designated area of an establishment in a municipality that voted to allow on-premises consumption; the subsection does not limit medical use. - (d) Open container in the passenger area of a motor vehicle: up to $500 (trunk/locked glove compartment excluded). - (e) (as amended 2026) A person 21+ cultivating more than 6 but not more than 12 plants, or possessing outside a residence more than 2 oz but not more than 3 oz: only a civil penalty up to $100 + forfeiture of the excess. - (f) Under-21 procurement: up to $100 + drug awareness programme (c.94C §32M). (i) Furnishing marijuana to a person under 21: fine up to $2,000 and/or up to 1 year imprisonment. §13(i) does not apply to the sale, delivery or furnishing of medical marijuana under c.94I, and "furnish" excludes allowing one's own children and grandchildren to possess marijuana on premises or property owned or controlled by the person charged.

Daily retail purchase limit and equivalency — CCC Administrative Order No. 6, issued/effective April 19, 2026 — Verify (order and 935 CMR 500.140(3) contents below: confirm by opening the order and the CCC-posted regulation PDF. The statutory basis is Confirmed: Ch.65 §24 adds c.94G §4(a½)(xxxix) directing the CCC to set equivalency/conversion standards considering potency and form. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23). Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/04/administrative-order-relative-to-equivalency-and-conversion-standards-for-marijuana-products-april-19-2026/ — Accessed 2026-10-23:

"a Marijuana Retailer may not knowingly sell more than two ounces of Marijuana or its combined dry weight equivalent in Marijuana concentrate or Edibles to a retail customer per day. 1. Two ounces of Marijuana flower shall be equivalent to 10 grams of active THC in Marijuana concentrate including… Tinctures. 2. Two ounces… equivalent to 1,000 milligrams of active THC in Edibles."

The June 18, 2026 emergency regulations (935 CMR 500.140(3)) state the per-ounce equivalency (5 g concentrate / 500 mg edibles per ounce), provide that "Topicals and ointments shall not be subject to a limitation on daily sales," require the point-of-sale system to block excess transactions, and prohibit retailers from selling alcohol or tobacco/nicotine products. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Edibles dosing — 935 CMR 500.150(4) / 501.150 — Verify (confirm from the official 935 CMR 500.150 text via the State Bookstore / CCC-posted PDF and 500.160(12); amendment history below derived from Cornell LII annotations is not a primary source). The original (2019) text provided: "(a) For a single serving… five milligrams (5.00 mg) of active THC; (b) In a single package of multiple Edible… not more than 20 servings or 100 milligrams (100.00mg) of active THC; (c) THC content must be homogenous…". The amended text effective November 22, 2024 reads: "(a) For a single serving of an Edible, 5.5 milligrams (5.50 mg)… (b) In a single package… not more than 20 servings or 110 milligrams (110.00mg)" — expressly "includes the 10% variance allowed by 935 CMR 500.160(12)." So the operative rule is: nominal limit 5 mg per serving / 100 mg per package, i.e. 5.5 mg / 110 mg when the 10% testing variance is included. Sources: CCC-posted 935 CMR 500.150 texts (2019 and as amended eff. 2024-11-22), via https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. Amendment history (per Cornell LII annotations): adopted Mass. Register 1361 eff. 2018-03-23; amended 1403 eff. 2019-11-01; 1434/1441 eff. 2021-01-08; 501.150 also amended 1507 eff. 2023-10-27 and 1535 eff. 2024-11-22.

Beverages — Verify (confirm in the CCC-posted regulation PDF). Emergency regulations: "Packaging for Marijuana Product Beverages shall be packaged solely in a single serving size. Except as otherwise provided in 935 CMR 500.141(3), multiple-serving Beverages are strictly prohibited for sale to Consumers," and "in no instance shall an individual serving size of any Marijuana Product contain more than five milligrams of delta-nine-tetrahydrocannabinol (Δ9-THC) subject to the testing variance specified in 935 CMR 500.160(12)." Source: 935 CMR 500 (eff. 2026-06-18), above — Accessed 2026-10-23. Vapes and concentrates are lawful product categories sold subject to the equivalency limits above (vape cartridges were 18.4% and concentrates 7.6% of sales Nov 2018–Apr 2024 per the CCC Industry Report, Part 6).

General operational requirements — MGL c.94G §12. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section12 — Accessed 2026-10-23: secure entrances and inventory control; operations only at a CCC-approved enclosed address; nothing visible from a public place without optical aids; CCC inspection/audit access; no workers under 21; §12(f) bars products with cannabinoids other than those produced/distributed/taxed under c.94G or hemp cultivated/manufactured under an MDAR licence under c.128 and USDA-compliant; operations certificate required; emergency plan filed with host-community fire and police departments.

1.4 Medical programme (MGL c.94I; 935 CMR 501.000)

Programme structure — c.94I §2 (current, Ch.65-amended text). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94I/Section2 — Accessed 2026-10-23. The CCC operates the programme. A qualifying patient with a debilitating condition may obtain written/electronic certification from a healthcare professional with whom they have a bona fide relationship; the CCC issues a registration card; an MME may sell to card holders. Healthcare professionals, patients and caregivers acting within the chapter are immune from state prosecution/penalty. §2(d) provides a cultivation registration where access is limited by verified financial hardship, physical incapacity, or no MME within a reasonable distance — limited plants "sufficient to maintain a 60-day supply," in an enclosed, locked area only. The criteria in §2(d) as amended (Ch.65 §47 changed only the MTC→MME wording) are "verified financial hardship, a physical incapacity to access reasonable transportation or the lack of a medical marijuana establishment within a reasonable distance."

Qualifying conditions — list PLUS provider discretion (not a closed list). The named list is Confirmed at https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ (ALS, Cancer, Crohn's, Glaucoma, HIV/AIDS, Hepatitis C, MS, Parkinson's); the verbatim 935 CMR 501.002 definition and provider-discretion clause below are Verify (confirm in the CCC-posted 501 PDF; Know the Laws alone does not state provider discretion). 935 CMR 501.002 defines:

"Debilitating Medical Condition means cancer, glaucoma, positive status for human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), acquired immune deficiency syndrome (AIDS), hepatitis C, amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS), Crohn's disease, Parkinson's disease, and multiple sclerosis (MS), when such diseases are debilitating, and other debilitating conditions as determined in writing by a Qualifying Patient's healthcare provider."

"Debilitating" means causing weakness, cachexia, wasting syndrome, intractable pain or nausea, or impairing strength/ability, progressing so that at least one major life activity is substantially limited. The CCC "Know the Laws" page lists the same named conditions (ALS, Cancer, Crohn's Disease, Glaucoma, HIV/AIDS, Hepatitis C, MS, Parkinson's). Sources: CCC-posted 935 CMR 501.002 definitions; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Card process. Patients must be 18+ Massachusetts residents diagnosed by a Certifying Healthcare Provider. Per CCC "Know the Laws," qualifying patients under 18 must be approved by two Massachusetts-licensed certifying physicians, who must diagnose a debilitating life-limiting illness or condition; the further pinpoints that one of the two must be a board-certified paediatrician or paediatric subspecialist, and the parental-consent and neurology-risk details (cited to a CCC certifying-provider page), are Verify. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. No patient registration fee — Verify (confirm on the Mass.gov/CCC medical programme page). Patients may possess up to a 60-day supply (CCC "Know the Laws"). Telehealth for initial certification was allowed by the 2024–25 regulation reforms — Verify against the current 501 text before relying on the detail. Certification duration and caregiver ratios were not verified from the current 501 text in this run — Verify.

Out-of-state reciprocity — CONFLICTING official CCC sources; no general reciprocity. The CCC "Know the Laws" page states: "Out-of-state residents cannot purchase medical marijuana in Massachusetts. Only Registered Qualifying Patients who are residents of Massachusetts or their Caregivers…" However, the 935 CMR 501.002 definition of "Qualifying Patient" includes "(a) a Massachusetts Resident or a non-Massachusetts Resident receiving end-of-life or palliative care or cancer treatment in Massachusetts as determined by a Certifying Healthcare Provider, who is 18 years of age or older…". Both are official CCC sources; they are reported here as written and flagged Verify/conflicting. No recognition of other states' medical cards was found. Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ ; CCC-posted 935 CMR 501 text — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Medical home grow — CONFLICTING CCC presentation; statute controls. The CCC "Know the Laws" page states registered patients may grow "up to twelve flowering and twelve vegetative cannabis plants in your home," separately describes hardship cultivation for a 60-day supply, and states "The Commission has not yet implemented the Hardship Cultivation Registration program." The statute (c.94I §2(d), above) authorises cultivation only via the hardship-type cultivation registration. Flagged Verify; rely on the statute. Note the internal CCC tension: Know the Laws simultaneously describes a general patient 12+12 home-grow right that has no basis in the c.94I §2(d) text.

Medical licensee. The only MME type currently authorised is the Fully Integrated MTC (FIMTC) — vertically integrated cultivation, processing and retail sale of its own products for medical use, plus delivery to Registered Qualifying Patients. Ch.65 §51 removed simultaneous cultivate/process/dispense as a condition of licensure (new c.94I §6(iv): the chapter shall not "require medical marijuana establishments to simultaneously cultivate, process and dispense… as a condition of licensure"). Under Ch.65 §61, licences for non-FIMTC MME types are exclusive to social equity businesses for 24 months from the first notice to commence operations, extendable — so new non-FIMTC types are social-equity-exclusive for at least 24 months. Whether only the FIMTC type is currently operating is Verify (confirm at https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-types/). Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-types/ ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

1.5 Hemp (MGL c.128 §§116–123; 330 CMR 32.00; MDAR)

State definition — c.128 §116 (verbatim). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXIX/Chapter128/Section116 — Accessed 2026-10-23:

"'Hemp', the plant of the genus cannabis and any part of the plant, whether growing or not, with a delta-9-tetrahydrocannabinol concentration that does not exceed 0.3 per cent on a dry weight basis or per volume or weight of marijuana product or the combined per cent of delta-9-tetrahydrocannabinol and tetrahydrocannabinolic acid in any part of the plant of the genus cannabis regardless of moisture content."

So Massachusetts already measures hemp by a combined Δ9-THC + THCA standard, not delta-9 alone.

MDAR programme. MDAR is the sole hemp licensing authority in Massachusetts, under 330 CMR 32.00 (see §1.2). The USDA approved the Massachusetts State Plan on May 7, 2020 (updated December 20, 2021). Testing is by HPLC; total THC is computed as "delta-9 THC + (THCa * 0.877)"; MDAR tests the crop before harvest and it must be <0.3% THC. Licence fees per the MDAR FAQ table: Grower $100 non-refundable application + $300 licence; Processor $100 + $300; Dual Grower/Processor $100 + $500. Renewals run October 1–December 1 with a $100 renewal application fee and an FBI Identity History requirement for producer/dual key participants — Verify (confirm on the MDAR licensing page / 330 CMR 32.00). Hemp (unlike marijuana) receives the agricultural protection of MGL c.40A §3. MDAR's Processor Policy dates (originally issued 2018-04-30, updated April 3, 2024) — Verify: https://www.mass.gov/policy-statement/massachusetts-hemp-program-processor-policy . Sources: https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs ; https://www.mass.gov/regulations/330-CMR-3200-hemp-production — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Sale of hemp-derived products — MDAR regulates wholesale only; no retail licence is needed to sell hemp-derived products. Source: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth — Accessed 2026-10-23. - May be wholesaled: hemp seed, seed oil, hulled seed, seed powder, protein; clothing, building material and fibre items; non-food CBD products without medicinal/therapeutic claims and not marketed as a dietary supplement unless FDA-approved; flower/plant material licensed grower → grower/processor only. - May NOT be wholesaled: any food product containing CBD; CBD products with therapeutic/medicinal claims (unless FDA-approved); CBD marketed as a dietary supplement (unless FDA-approved); animal feed with hemp/CBD; unprocessed/raw plant hemp including flower meant for end use by a consumer (retail raw flower is barred; MDAR's stated rationale is distinguishing hemp from marijuana and lot-average testing). - Delta-8 (verbatim, MDAR): "Because delta-8 THC is not naturally occurring in hemp (except for possible trace amounts), to produce delta-8 THC in commercial quantities it must be derived from hemp synthetically… delta-8 THC remains a controlled substance, regardless of the source. As a result, we do not allow hemp-derived delta-8 THC products to be processed or sold in Massachusetts." - Food: DPH rules bar adding CBD/THC to food or beverages in the ordinary food channel — Verify (DPH food pinpoint: confirm on mass.gov); hulled hemp seed, hemp seed protein and hemp seed oil are GRAS per the FDA evaluations noted by MDAR. The ABCC's May 30, 2024 advisory applies the same position on alcohol-licensed premises (CCC-licensed marijuana edibles are exempt from that notice) — Verify (ABCC advisory pinpoint: confirm on mass.gov). The bar on general-retail intoxicating hemp products follows from the MDAR wholesale bar above. - THC drinks (hemp-derived, general retail): effectively barred under the current DPH food rules + MDAR wholesale rules above. No lawful general-retail intoxicating hemp beverage regime has been enacted as of October 2026 (bills proposing one are pending or were sent to study — §1.9). CCC-licensed marijuana beverages are a separate channel (single-serving, ≤5 mg Δ9-THC per serving — §1.3).

Federal change — PL 119-37 §781 (enacted text). Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ37/html/PLAW-119publ37.htm — Accessed 2026-10-23. PL 119-37 was enacted/signed November 12, 2025; §781 amends the federal hemp definition to a "total [THC] concentration (including tetrahydrocannabinolic acid [(THCA)]) of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis" (replacing the delta-9-only standard of the 2018 Farm Bill, 7 U.S.C. §1639o). It excludes: viable seeds from plants >0.3% total THC; hemp-derived cannabinoid products with cannabinoids that are (1) not capable of being naturally produced, or (2) capable of being naturally produced but synthesised/manufactured outside the plant; intermediate products >0.3% total THC (plus HHS-determined similar-effect cannabinoids); and final products containing more than 0.4 mg combined total per container of total THC (incl. THCA) plus similar-effect cannabinoids. Industrial hemp (stalks, fibres, whole grains, oils, edible greens for non-cannabinoid products) is expressly included. FDA must publish cannabinoid lists and "container" specificity within 90 days of enactment — Verify (§781(3) pinpoint: confirm at the GovInfo URL above). The amendment provides it is "Effective 365 days after the enactment of this Act," i.e. November 12, 2026 (the April 2026 Federal Register order's footnote 11 confirms the same revised-definition date).

Delay — PL 119-103 §2019. PL 119-103 (H.R.6500, Continuing Appropriations and Extensions Act, 2027, signed September 2, 2026) §2019, "Section 781 Extension," provides that until December 11, 2026 the §781 amendments apply only to intermediate/final products described in (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I) of 7 U.S.C. §1639o (as amended by §781) — i.e. products containing cannabinoids not capable of being naturally produced by Cannabis sativa L. The broader total-THC standard, the synthesised-outside-the-plant exclusion, the intermediate 0.3% cap and the final 0.4 mg/container cap therefore do not apply until December 11, 2026 on the enacted text. §2019 does not move §781's November 12, 2026 effective date; it narrows its application until December 11, 2026. Source (enacted text): https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ103/html/PLAW-119publ103.htm — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Massachusetts applicability. The federal redefinition does not itself amend MGL c.128 §116 (which already uses a combined Δ9+THCa measure) or the MDAR delta-8/food rules. Whether products reclassified as federal "marijuana" would fall under the federal Controlled Substances Act is Unverified on the §781/§2019 text. No Massachusetts statutory/regulatory response beyond the Ch.65 §64 CCC study of intoxicating hemp regulation (due December 15, 2026) was found on an official Massachusetts source as of October 2026.

1.6 Packaging, labelling, advertising, delivery, zoning/buffers

Packaging and labelling — 935 CMR 500.105(5)–(6) — Verify (label contents, symbol/scoring rules and the $50 preapproval fee below: confirm in the CCC-posted 935 CMR 500 text and https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-fees/). Labels must be legible and affixed, in type at least 1/16 inch, showing producer name, registration number, phone and email; packager and date; batch/serial/barcode; net weight/volume in US customary and metric units; and the full cannabinoid profile including THC. Single servings within multi-serving packages must be marked/stamped with the CCC symbol; solid edibles must be scored to identify servings, and products that cannot be scored may be sold only as single servings. Packaging/labelling preapproval costs $50 per product (CCC fee schedule, Part 4). Source: 935 CMR 500 (eff. 2026-06-18) and https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-fees/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. Specific child-resistant/opaque/plain-packaging wording was not quoted from the full 500.105(6) text in this run — Verify before stating specifics.

Advertising — 935 CMR 500.105(4) — Verify (prohibited list below: confirm in the CCC-posted regulation text). The regulation's prohibited list includes advertising price (except as permitted), free promotional items including "gifts, giveaways, discounts, points-based reward systems, customer loyalty programs, coupons, and 'free' or 'donated' Marijuana" (except brand-name take-away bags after purchase), transport-venue advertising, and exterior displays visible from outside, among other restrictions. Ch.65 §21 (statute) now allows the CCC to permit sales/discount/loyalty advertising within an establishment or via an opt-in email list only; a CCC bulletin of September 25, 2026 was reported (secondary) as clarifying that Ch.65 did not authorise website discount advertising — Verify against the official bulletin. Sources: 935 CMR 500 (eff. 2026-06-18); https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Delivery. Adult-use delivery types: Marijuana Courier, Marijuana Delivery Operator, and Marijuana Establishments with a Delivery Endorsement (935 CMR 500.145/500.146; fees in Part 4). Post-Ch.65, residential delivery is permitted in all municipalities unless a municipality without authorised retailers obtains a CCC delivery-prohibition waiver (≤2 years, extendable); medical delivery by MMEs is permitted by law in all 351 cities and towns. The delivery mechanism is a CCC-granted waiver on municipal request, available only to a municipality that does not authorise retail licences — not a general opt-out. Delivery hours are 7 a.m.–11 p.m. unless a municipality narrows them by bylaw/ordinance — Verify (confirm in 935 CMR 500.145/500.146). Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-types/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Zoning and the 500-foot school buffer. State framework: municipalities control time/place/manner under c.94G §3 (Part 3). The Mass.gov law page summarises the rule as: a dispensary may not be within 500 feet of a school. Fuller regulatory text (older CCC/Boston materials) states the application must confirm the establishment is not within 500 ft of a pre-existing public/private school providing K-12 education unless the municipality has adopted an ordinance/bylaw reducing the distance, and that, absent local requirements, 935 CMR 500.110 sets a fallback of 500 ft from a school, daycare centre, or facility where children commonly congregate. Municipalities may be stricter (e.g., Chicopee's local code cites 500.110(3) plus parks, churches, libraries, playgrounds and youth centres). Exact current wording of 500.110 in the June 2026 emergency text: Verify. Sources: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/massachusetts-law-about-recreational-marijuana ; 935 CMR 500 (eff. 2026-06-18) — Accessed 2026-10-23.

1.7 Seed-to-sale tracking — Metrc (verified)

Yes — Metrc is the Commonwealth's seed-to-sale system of record. The CCC Sales & Product Distribution page states: "All Cannabis Control Commission (Commission) licensees must register and trace their products using the Commonwealth's seed-to-sale tracking technology of record," and a CCC January 2026 release states sales totals are "based on data input by licensees into the state's seed-to-sale system of record, Metrc." The CCC Product Catalog is hosted through Metrc, and licensees pay monthly Metrc programme fees plus tag fees (waivers in Part 4). Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/open-data/sales-and-product-distribution/ ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-fees/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

1.8 Employment and drug testing

  • No general adult-use off-duty employment protection was found in the current c.94G text. The CCC "Know the Laws" page states: "Employers, landlords, cities, and towns may have their own policies governing the use of marijuana." Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • A bill to create such protection is pending, not law: H.2179 (§1.9).
  • Medical off-duty accommodation under disability law/case law, unemployment-insurance treatment of off-duty use, and DOT/federal testing rules were encountered only in secondary sources in this run — Verify; no official primary text was opened, and secondary summaries are not relied on for law here.
  • Expungement/sealing: the blanket pardon is at Part 0, item 4. The statutory marijuana-expungement provisions (MGL c.276 §100K / §100K¼) were not opened from official text in this run — Verify. (Mass.gov's law page links the Trial Court's petition-for-expungement form for marijuana offences and the Probation petition-to-seal form.)

Part 2 — Taxes

All sources official (DOR / malegislature.gov), accessed 2026-10-23.

Tax Rate Basis / notes
Adult-use excise (MGL c.64N §2) 10.75% of total sales price Imposed on sale by a marijuana retailer to anyone other than a marijuana establishment
State sales tax (MGL c.64H §2, via c.64N §2) 6.25% Levied in addition to the excise
Local option (MGL c.64N §3) Up to 3% of total sales price Only in a city/town that has accepted §3; collected by DOR and distributed at least quarterly to the town
Combined maximum 20.00% Arithmetic sum of the three rates above
Medical marijuana (MGL c.94I §8) Exempt Not taxed under cc.64H, 64I, 64K or 64N
County cannabis tax None found The local tax is municipal (city/town) only

c.64N §2 (verbatim): "An excise tax is hereby imposed upon the sale of marijuana or marijuana products by a marijuana retailer to anyone other than a marijuana establishment at a rate of 10.75 per cent of the total sales price… levied in addition to state tax… as provided in section 2 of chapter 64H… paid by a marijuana retailer to the commissioner…" History per Mass.gov: added St. 2016, c.334 §4; amended St. 2017, c.55 §12 (eff. 2017-07-28), St. 2022, c.180 §5 (eff. 2022-11-09; c.180 §5 added the 1% social-equity distribution paragraph now in c.64N §5), St. 2022, c.268 §107 (eff. 2022-11-10) — the c.268 §107 pinpoint is Verify; last updated 2022-11-10. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleIX/Chapter64N/Section2 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

c.64N §3 (local option): a city or town accepting §3 may impose a local sales tax "at a rate not greater than 3 per cent of the total sales price," paid to the commissioner like the state sales tax and distributed at least quarterly to the town in proportion to the sums received there; effective the first day of a calendar quarter after 30 days post-acceptance. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleIX/Chapter64N/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23. Under c.64N §5, "all monies… equal to 1 per cent of the total sales price… from that portion of the excise imposed by section 2 from a marijuana retailer that is a social equity business" are distributed at least quarterly to towns with at least one social equity retailer, pro rata; §5 also provides that c.64N revenue other than the c.64H §2 sales tax is deposited in the Marijuana Regulation Fund. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleIX/Chapter64N/Section5 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Filing (DOR Form MRT). That a single return covers all three taxes is Confirmed by the DOR FAQ: "Only one return is necessary… sales tax, excise tax, and local option tax on the same Form MRT Return" (https://www.mass.gov/info-details/marijuana-retail-taxes-faqs — Accessed 2026-10-23). The line detail — Line 4 excise = taxable sales × 0.1075; Line 5 state sales tax = × 0.0625; Line 6 local = × the applicable local rate — and the DLS Municipal Databank list of local-option towns are Verify. Source: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/form-mrt-instructions-marijuana-retail-taxes-return — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Medical exemption mechanics (DOR FAQ): "In general, the sale of medical marijuana to a purchaser is not subject to tax, as long as the purchaser presents his or her Medical Use of Marijuana Program ID Card and valid government-issued identification at the time of sale," with the retailer verifying CCC certification/registration. Medical sales are not reported on the MRT return; accessories and other non-marijuana items go on the standard sales tax return. Wholesale sales are presumed retail unless an ST-4 resale or ST-12 exempt-use certificate is furnished. Source: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/marijuana-retail-taxes-faqs — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Delivery taxation (DOR FAQ): Delivery Operators and establishments with a delivery endorsement must register as DOR vendors and collect/remit the taxes; Couriers do not sell, so the retailer remits; the local option rate is the host community's rate for all deliveries by that operator/retailer. Source: same DOR FAQ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

IRC §280E decoupling — YES, Massachusetts decouples. DOR (verbatim): for tax years ending on or before 2021-12-31 Massachusetts followed IRC §280E; "'An Act Relative to Equity in the Cannabis Industry,' St. 2022, c. 180, §§ 1-2, provides that the personal income tax and corporate excise no longer follow Internal Revenue Code § 280E with respect to licensed marijuana businesses starting with tax years beginning on or after January 1, 2022. As a result, licensed marijuana businesses can deduct the trade or business deductions provided by the Internal Revenue Code in addition to the deduction for the cost of goods sold for Massachusetts tax purposes." The statute: c.180 §1 adds MGL c.62 §2(d)(4) (personal income tax — amounts "that would have been deductible under the Code, but for section 280E of said Code" for a c.94G marijuana establishment or a c.94I medical marijuana establishment [MME — c.180 as enacted in 2022 used "medical marijuana treatment center"; Ch.65 §§3–4 amended c.62 §2 and c.63 §30 to replace that term with "medical marijuana establishment"]) and §2 amends MGL c.63 §30 ¶4 (corporate excise). Sources: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/marijuana-retail-taxes-faqs ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2022/Chapter180 — Accessed 2026-10-23. Federal consequence of the April 2026 medical Schedule III order for §280E (which by its terms applies to Schedule I/II trafficking) was asserted only in secondary sources — Verify; no IRS/DOJ guidance was opened. The DOR FAQ's statement that marijuana "remains federally classified as a Schedule I controlled substance" is outdated for the Schedule III categories only; it remains accurate for adult-use marijuana.

Funds. Excise revenue flows to the Marijuana Regulation Fund (c.94G §14). Under c.94G §14(b), "annually, not later than January 1, an amount equal to 15 per cent of the revenue deposited in the fund in the prior fiscal year shall be transferred to the Cannabis Social Equity Trust Fund" (c.94G §14A, created by c.180; administered by the executive office of economic development under §14A(a) as amended by Ch.65 §33, renamed from "housing and economic development"). Sources: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section14 ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section14A — Accessed 2026-10-23. The community impact fee under an HCA (≤3% of gross sales, ≤8 years — Part 3) is not a tax and is kept distinct here.

Revenue by fiscal year — figures are labelled by year and tax basket; DOR is the authoritative source and the CCC states it does not collect tax revenue. The FY2025 state-only figure and the FY2026 state+local figure below are different years and different baskets and are not in conflict with each other. Only the two FY2025 state figures ($289M vs ~$280M) conflict as the same measure and remain Unverified — confirm at DOR. Report all as published: - CCC release, Jan 8, 2026: "$289 million in state tax revenue in fiscal year 2025, which ended June 30, according to data reported by the Department of Revenue. Generated off the 6.25% sales tax and 10.75% marijuana excise tax." https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. - CCC release, Feb 2026 ($9B milestone): cannabis sales and excise taxes generated "approximately $280 million in fiscal year 2025" per DOR — Unverified (release not opened in the audit pass). The official voter-guide fiscal statement likewise gives ~$280M state + ~$50M local for FY2025 — Unverified (confirm in the Secretary of Administration & Finance fiscal statement on the Secretary of the Commonwealth's Question 8 page and in DOR revenue reports on mass.gov). - CCC release, Sept 9, 2026: "cannabis sales, local, and excise taxes generated approximately $336 million in fiscal year 2026" per DOR; "Over $43 million in local option taxes to cities and towns in fiscal year 2026 alone"; "$2 billion in state and local taxes generated since 2016." https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. - FY2024 ($272M = $173.7M excise + $98.7M sales) was seen only in a secondary synthesis citing official data — Verify. DOR's own monthly revenue reports/Blue Book were not opened in this run — Unavailable from DOR directly in this run.

Cannabis and Hemp in Massachusetts, USA — Corrected Parts 3–6

Date accessed for all sources: 2026-10-23. Every fact below carries its source link and that access date. Facts are drawn from official sources opened directly (Massachusetts General Laws at malegislature.gov, Cannabis Control Commission [CCC], Mass.gov/DOR/MDAR) wherever possible. Anything uncertain, conflicting, or not found on an official source is flagged Verify or Unavailable in line.

Part 3 — Local Rules

3.1 Level of government: municipal, not county

Massachusetts sets cannabis local control at the municipal (city/town) level under MGL c.94G §3. Counties have no licensing or zoning role found; the only county-specific state provisions are the CCC's special regulations for Dukes and Nantucket Counties (935 CMR 500.200, e.g. transport over water). Massachusetts has 351 municipalities. Sources: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

c.94G §3 (operative provisions, paraphrase with key verbatim limits): - (a) Cities/towns may adopt reasonable time/place/manner ordinances and bylaws that are not unreasonably impracticable and do not conflict with state law. Under §3(a)(2), a bylaw/ordinance that would (i) prohibit one or more types of establishment, (ii) limit retailers to fewer than 20% of c.138 §15 licences, or (iii) limit any type below the number of medical marijuana establishments registered for the same activity, must first be submitted to the voters under §3(e) — in Question-4-yes towns, and in ALL towns after 31 Dec 2019. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - (b) On-premises (social) consumption may be authorised by voter petition signed by 10% of voters at the preceding biennial state election, or by ordinance/bylaw without an initiative under §3(b)(3). Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - (c) No municipality may prohibit the transportation of marijuana or make it unreasonably impracticable. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23. - (d) An HCA is required for a new or renewal licence. The community impact fee must: "(A) be reasonably related to the costs imposed upon the municipality… as documented…; (B) amount to not more than 3 per cent of the gross sales…; (C) not be effective after… eighth year of operation; (D) commence on date… granted a final license…; (E) not mandate a certain percentage of total or gross sales." The fee encompasses all payments/obligations — an HCA may impose no additional monetary, in-kind or charitable obligations; costs must be documented and transmitted within one month after annual renewal and are a public record. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23.

3.2 Opt-in/opt-out, bans, caps, moratoriums, HCA reform timeline

  • The operative opt-in/opt-out mechanism today is the CCC Municipal Zoning Tracker (§3.3): municipalities show statuses including zoning in place (with or without licence/retail caps), bans, retail bans, and delivery-prohibition waivers under Ch.65.
  • HCA reform timeline: St. 2022, c.180 gave the CCC power to review/approve HCAs, issue a model HCA, and set minimum municipal equity standards; those rules are codified at 935 CMR 500.180 and 500.181, and municipalities had to adopt equity strategies/rules by May 1, 2024 — Host Communities must have adopted local rules or bylaws to comply with 935 CMR 500.181(3) and 501.181(3) on or before May 1, 2024. The statutory fee limits are in c.94G §3(d) (above). Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/chapter180/ ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • HCA administrative extensions: CCC Bulletin, 9 Oct 2026: at its 8 Oct 2026 meeting the Commission voted to continue authorising the Executive Director to approve administrative extensions of licence expiration dates to allow licensees more time to submit a compliant HCA or HCA Waiver. Effective 8 Oct 2026 new extensions require the licensee to be in good standing with DOR and to have filed all required tax returns (bulletin lists further conditions). Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/10/bulletin-administrative-extension-of-licenses-oct-9-2026/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Delivery bans post-Ch.65 operate by CCC waiver. As read in full on 23 Oct 2026, the Tracker shows 16 delivery-banned municipalities (waivers DW-2026-001 through DW-2026-016): Avon (DW-2026-007, exp. 10 Jun 2028), Carlisle (DW-2026-015, 15 Sep 2028), Foxborough (DW-2026-008, 17 Jun 2028), Hampden (DW-2026-003, 5 Jun 2028), Holden (DW-2026-006, 10 Jun 2028), Hopkinton (DW-2026-004, 8 Jun 2028), Lexington (DW-2026-012, 13 Jul 2027), Lincoln (DW-2026-005, 9 Jun 2027), Mattapoisett (DW-2026-013, 13 Jul 2028), Medfield (DW-2026-010, 26 Jun 2028), Middleton (DW-2026-009, 26 Jun 2028), Needham (DW-2026-002, 5 Jun 2028), North Andover (DW-2026-014, 7 Aug 2028), Spencer (DW-2026-016, 23 Sep 2027), Wayland (DW-2026-011, 26 Jun 2028), Westborough (DW-2026-001, 5 Jun 2028). Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Ch.65 delivery waiver: valid ≤2 years, extendable in 2-year increments — "said waiver shall be valid for not more than 2 years and may be extended in 2-year increments upon request, at the commission's discretion." Note: Lexington, Lincoln and Spencer expiries are shorter than 2 years — CCC granted shorter terms in those cases. Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Social consumption: All 351 rows of the Tracker (read in full, 23 Oct 2026) show Social Consumption Status = "No zoning in place". No municipality had opted in / zoned for social consumption as of that date. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

3.3 Official jurisdiction list — CCC Municipal Zoning Tracker

Exact URL: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. The tracker lists all 351 municipalities, each with a link to the town website and three status columns: Adult Use Status, Delivery Status, Social Consumption Status. The page carries this disclaimer: the tracker is "intended as a general reference resource and does not constitute an official or comprehensive legal determination of local laws." Statuses used include: "Zoning in place"; "Zoning in place; license cap"; "Zoning in place; retail license cap"; "Ban in place"; "Retail ban"; "Zoning in place; retail ban"; "No zoning in place"; "No response"; "Zoning in place (litigation pending)" (Charlton); and "Zoning in Place; retail ban" (Stow).

Tracker header (verbatim substance): "Pursuant to Chapter 65 of the Acts of 2026, adult-use marijuana delivery is permitted statewide unless a municipality that does not authorize Marijuana Retailers has obtained a waiver from the Commission to prohibit delivery… Delivery of medical marijuana by Medical Marijuana Establishments is permitted by law in all 351 cities and towns."

Examples observed directly in the tracker: Boston, Cambridge, Brookline, Brockton — "Zoning in place," delivery permitted. Amherst, Ayer, Chelsea — zoning in place with licence cap. Acton, Andover, Ashland, Bedford, Braintree, Canton — "Ban in place," delivery permitted. Avon — ban + "Delivery banned DW-2026-007 Expiry: June 10, 2028"; Carlisle — ban + "Delivery banned DW-2026-015 Expiry: September 15, 2028"; Foxborough — ban + "Delivery banned DW-2026-008 Expiry: June 17, 2028." The full 351-row table was not reproduced in this run; use the tracker URL above as the official list.

3.4 Sampled municipal fees and requirements (official municipal sources only)

  • Boston. Local licensing runs through the Boston Cannabis Board plus an HCA with the City. The BCB's Buffer Zone Attestation form (version dated 2025-04-16) states the Boston Zoning Code requires a cannabis establishment to be "at least one half mile or 2,640 feet from another existing cannabis establishment and at least 500 feet from a preexisting public or private school providing education in kindergarten or grades 1–12." Note: the measurement method differs between form versions (2025: entrance-to-entrance straight line — the 2025 form measures entrance-centre to entrance-centre, with a pedestrian-path exception where an impassable barrier intervenes; 2021: lot-line-to-lot-line). A Boston Plans draft text amendment proposing removal of the half-mile buffer was found — a proposal only. Current Boston Zoning Code Article 8 text: Verify. Boston's local licence fee amount: Unavailable (not found on an official source in this run). Sources: https://www.boston.gov/sites/default/files/file/2025/05/Buffer%20Zone%20Attestation%20BCB%204.16.25%20%281%29.pdf ; https://www.boston.gov/sites/default/files/file/2022/02/Buffer Zone Attestation BCB (updated 1.31.2022)_0.pdf ; https://www.bostonplans.org/getattachment/0b44ae25-142e-4495-a8db-993ac1619e92 ; https://www.boston.gov/departments/licensing-board/boston-cannabis-board — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Cambridge. A Cannabis Retail Store shall not be permitted within one thousand and eight hundred (1,800) feet of another Cannabis Retail Store — §11.803.1(b): "A Cannabis Retail Store shall not be permitted within one thousand and eight hundred (1,800) feet of another Cannabis Retail Store" except EEA/SEP designees — plus a Planning Board special permit under Zoning Article 11.800, and a 300-ft school/youth-facility buffer in §11.800 materials. Removal of the buffer/special-permit requirement was debated 2023–2025; the June 2023 CDD presentation proposing removal is a proposal document and no enacted removal was confirmed from the current Cambridge Zoning Ordinance text. Current Cambridge zoning text and local fees were not opened from the official code in this run — Verify / fees Unavailable. Sources: https://www.cambridgema.gov/-/media/Files/CDD/ZoningDevel/Amendments/2021/article22,-d-,000emissions/zngamend_CannabisDelivery_TextRevisions_20210628.pdf ; https://www.cambridgema.gov/cdd/zoninganddevelopment/zoning — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Somerville. The municipal code (Ordinance §2-221) establishes a priority-applicant licensing system (Groups A/B) with the priority period extended to 5 years — seen via the official code host (encodeplus) search result but not opened directly: Verify. Local fee amounts: Unavailable. Source: https://online.encodeplus.com/regs/somerville-ma-coo/doclibrary.aspx?id=10f3ec50-f23e-46d4-98f3-069f304e9d50 — Accessed 2026-10-23 (search-level only).
  • Northampton. The first adult-use retailers in Massachusetts opened in Northampton and Leicester in November 2018 (CCC). Northampton's local zoning and fee schedule were not retrieved from an official city source in this run — Unavailable. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. (A 2026 Board of Health discussion of social-consumption opt-in was reported only secondarily; it is not a decision and no opt-in is stated.)

3.5 Zones and buffer differences

The state baseline is the 500-ft school buffer (§1.6), which municipalities may reduce by ordinance/bylaw for schools and may expand to other sensitive uses (e.g., Chicopee adds parks, churches, libraries, playgrounds, youth centres — Verify: Chicopee's specific added uses were not re-opened from Chicopee's official code) or supplement with establishment-to-establishment buffers (Boston's half-mile rule, §3.4; Cambridge's 1,800-ft rule and 300-ft school/youth-facility buffer, §3.4). Zoning districts themselves are set town-by-town; the CCC tracker (§3.3) is the official statewide pointer to each municipality's position. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

3.6 Business concentration — official CCC sources only

From the CCC's February 2025 Industry Report release (official): "Across all municipalities, there was an average of 5.1 Marijuana Retailers per 100,000 residents… Saturation of cannabis retailers was highest in the western counties of Berkshire (18.9) and Hampshire (16.6) and was lowest in Norfolk (1.4) and Middlesex (3.7). Density of retailers with RMD/MTC density was highest in Nantucket (6.9) and Hampshire (6.8) counties, and lowest in Hampden and Norfolk (0.7) counties." Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2025/02/cannabis-control-commission-releases-industry-report-assessing-adult-and-medical-use-cannabis-markets/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. No other concentration claims are made here; no clusters were estimated.

Part 4 — Licence Types, Fees, Social Equity

Authoritative sources (opened): CCC License Types page https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-types/ and CCC License Fees page https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-fees/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. Fees below are application fee / initial & annual licence fee. All fees are non-refundable and cannot be waived except as stated in "Fee waivers" below.

4.3 Other fees and waivers (CCC fees page)

Change of Name $1,000; Change of Location — adult-use 50% of the licence fee, medical $10,000; Building Structure change $1,000; Change of Ownership/Control $5,000 per entity per licence / $500 per person (individuals); Architectural Review $1,500; Packaging & Labelling Preapproval $50 per product; plus background-check, fingerprinting, monthly Metrc programme fees, Metrc tag fees, and agent registration fees. Waivers: Economic Empowerment Priority / Social Equity Program / Disadvantaged Business Enterprises (WBE/MBE/VBE certified by the Supplier Diversity Office and meeting the CCC Small Business definition) and Microbusinesses — 100% of application fees, 50% of initial/annual licence fees, and monthly Metrc programme fees waived (tags not waived); Craft Co-ops — Metrc monthly fees waived; Couriers/Delivery Operators — 100% reduction of the first licence fee after provisional approval, then 50% on renewals. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/license-fees/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

4.4 Caps, windows, moratorium

There is no general application-window system: applications are rolling via MassCIP — Verify (no CCC primary page stating a general "rolling applications via MassCIP" rule was opened; confirm at CCC Guidance on Licensure / MassCIP portal), except: (i) the delivery and social-consumption exclusivity periods above; (ii) the ownership caps in c.94G §16; (iii) municipal caps/bans (Part 3); and (iv) the cultivator moratorium to June 30, 2027.

Ownership caps, c.94G §16 (Ch.65): max 6 retailer / 3 FIMTC / 3 product manufacturer / 3 cultivator; <20% financial interest without control exempt; ESOP trustees exempt. Phase-in: during the first 12 months after the CCC begins accepting applications under Ch.65, a 6th retailer licence is available only to Social Equity Businesses; non-SEB licensees are capped at 5 retailer licences in that period; thereafter the cap is 6 for all. Also, under §16, a holder of 3 FIMTC licences "shall not hold any additional medical marijuana establishment licenses" under c.94I §7. Sources: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section16 ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/07/cannabis-control-commission-seeks-feedback-on-chapter-65-emergency-regulations/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

Cultivator moratorium: CCC bulletin 9 Oct 2026; first approved 16 Apr 2026, discussed 11 June 2026; covers Indoor/Outdoor Cultivator applications and non-majority-equity Microbusiness applications; does NOT apply to Microbusiness applications majority equity-owned by eligible SEP participants or Economic Empowerment Applicants. "This suspension will remain in effect until June 30, 2027. The Commission may shorten or extend…" Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/10/bulletin-temporary-moratorium-of-marijuana-cultivator-license-applications-oct-9-2026/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

4.5 Social Equity Program and Economic Empowerment (official CCC)

Legal basis: MGL c.94G §22 (Social Equity Program) and §14A (Cannabis Social Equity Trust Fund, funded by 15% of Marijuana Regulation Fund revenues). Ch.65 defines a Social Equity Business as one ≥51% owned by SEP-eligible individuals or with EE-qualifying ownership: "not less than 51 per cent majority ownership of individuals who are eligible for the social equity program under section 22 or whose ownership qualifies it as an economic empowerment priority applicant." SEP eligibility (CCC, August 2026 opening release) requires at least one of: (1) household income ≤400% of Area Median Income and residence for ≥5 of the past 10 years in an Area of Disproportionate Impact (ADI); (2) MA resident ≥12 months with a c.94C conviction/CWOF (or equivalent in another jurisdiction); (3) MA resident ≥12 months who is the spouse/child of such a person; or (4) recognition as an original Economic Empowerment Applicant meeting EE criteria (ADI residence, service experience, Black/African American/Hispanic/Latino descent, or other articulable empowerment demonstration). The 2026 SEP application window ran 1 Sep – 30 Nov 2026, with virtual clinics from 26 Aug. Applications are via MassCIP; review typically takes 2–3 months — Verify (the 2–3 month review-time figure appears in no primary source opened). Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/08/massachusetts-cannabis-control-commission-opens-social-equity-program-to-new-applicants/ ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/equity/getting-started/ ; https://malegislature.gov/Laws/SessionLaws/Acts/2026/Chapter65 — Accessed 2026-10-23. Scale (CCC, September 2026 release): the SEP has accepted nearly 1,300 participants; over 100 equity-owned/operated businesses are operating; and "Almost $60 million in financial assistance to equity licenses since 2024 funded by cannabis tax revenue through the Social Equity Trust Fund." Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

4.6 Startup costs

No official CCC startup-cost figures exist — Unavailable. No third-party ranges are presented here, because none were verified from an official source in this run; any such figures used elsewhere must be labelled non-official estimates.

Part 5 — Licence Data Sources (inventory; individual holders are NOT listed)

5.1 CCC Open Data Catalog — the central source

URL: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/open-data/data-catalog/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. The CCC states that all Open Data is self-reported by licensees and that it does not guarantee completeness, accuracy or timeliness; each file carries a "Last Updated" column (CCCLastUpdated). Most datasets are offered as a .csv download plus a .json API endpoint. No XLSX/PDF versions of the core licence/sales datasets were offered on the catalog page.

Dataset groups on the catalog: - Applications & Licenses: Marijuana Establishment License Application DBE Totals; ME License Applications — Approved, Pending, Re-Opened; Total Adult-use ME License Applications Submitted; Adult-Use ME Licenses — Commence Ops; Medical Marijuana Establishments (MMEs); All Licenses/Licensing Tracker for Adult-Use and MMEs. - Sales: ME Facility Sales & Statistics (by day and product type, 2018–current); Marijuana Retail & Delivery Weekly Sales Report Total; Average Monthly Price per Ounce for Adult-Use Cannabis; MME Facility Sales; Adult-Use + Medical Plant Activity & Volume. - Agents: Active ME Agents Gender Totals; ME Agent Race/Ethnicity Totals. - Testing: e.g., "Test Results (THC, THCA, Arsenic, Cadmium, Lead, Mercury, Total Yeast & Mold) 01/01/2025–11/30/2025" — .csv 67 MB / .json 147 MB, Last update: 3/19/2026; "Test Results (THC, THCA, Microbials) 01/01/2024–12/31/2024" — .csv 99 MB / .json 269 MB, Last update: 4/15/2026. - 2025 Industry Report files: Adult Market Data Including Counties (csv); Facility Sales (csv); MTC Facility Statistics (csv); Average Monthly Price per Ounce (csv); Testing_Data_THC_THCA_Y&M-2021-2023 (csv); plus R Scripts.

Note on price datasets: Two CCC price series exist: the catalog's "Average Monthly Price per Ounce" dataset, and a per-gram series file (a_sales_au_price_per_gram.csv). Cite each by its exact filename/title; do not describe the per-gram file as "the catalog Price per Ounce dataset" (see §5.2).

Cite this file by its exact filename; do not describe it as "the catalog Price per Ounce dataset" CCCLastUpdated = 2026-10-20; latest month Sep 2026

Update frequency: the CCC does not state a fixed schedule on the catalog page; the files above were current to 2026-10-20 when accessed on 2026-10-23 (effectively continuously/regularly updated — stated frequency Verify). A full by-type licence count was not computed from the CSVs in this run (files were viewed in part only); official published counts are in Part 6.

Part 6 — Market Data (official CCC sources unless stated)

6.1 Sales

Source: CCC releases, accessed 2026-10-23. - CY2025 adult-use gross sales: over $1.65 billion — a new annual record, ~$3M above 2024 (so CY2024 ≈ $1.647B as implied by the CCC's comparison; the Feb 2025 Industry Report release separately describes 2024 as a "$1.64 billion sales record"). 46.3 million transactions in 2025 (+3.4M vs 2024). H1 2025: $806M. Cumulative sales crossed $8 billion at end of June 2025. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ - Cumulative adult-use gross sales crossed $10 billion on September 5, 2026 ($10,008,567,111 at announcement). 2026 year-to-date through August: $1.09 billion. June 2026: $156M — the highest single month ever. The $9 billion mark was passed in February 2026. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ - Historical (Industry Report, Feb 2025): sales from November 20, 2018 through April 30, 2024 totalled $6,063,229,651.92 (including $524,478,611.31 in 2024 to April 30). Product mix over that period: Buds 42.2%, Vape cartridges 18.4%, Pre-rolls 15.0%, Infused edibles and beverages 14.7%, Concentrates 7.6%. Year-over-year growth was largest 2018→2019 (+537%) and smallest 2022→2023 (+5%), with +7% 2023→2024. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2025/02/cannabis-control-commission-releases-industry-report-assessing-adult-and-medical-use-cannabis-markets/ - A full year-by-year 2018–2023 adult-use series and annual medical sales totals were not extracted from the CCC Facility Sales CSVs in this run — Unavailable/partial here (the data exist in the Part 5 catalog datasets).

6.2 Active businesses, agents, patients

  • 782 operating businesses (including over 100 equity-owned/operated) and 91 medical marijuana dispensaries — CCC, September 2026. More than 300 cities and towns were participating (hosting brick-and-mortar businesses or allowing delivery) as of September 2026. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • 416 Marijuana Retailers + Delivery Operators + Microbusinesses with delivery endorsements had notices to commence operations as of November 2025 (406 at the same point in 2024) — CCC, January 2026. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Delivery: 9 Delivery Couriers + 16 Delivery Operators combined for over $14M in 2026 YTD sales ($10.7M operators / $3.2M couriers) — CCC, September 2026. Delivery Operators grossed nearly $16.5M in CY2025 (+>$4M vs 2024) — CCC, January 2026. Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Agents: nearly 20,000 registered agents in adult-use as of August 2026, encompassing 15,000 individual employees (CCC, Sept 2026). The January 2026 release gives ~21,000 individual agents across adult-use and medical "to date" — different scope/date; both are cited as published. Sources: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/massachusetts-marijuana-establishments-hit-10-billion-in-gross-sales/ ; https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Patients: nearly 100,000 patients were registered with the Medical Use of Marijuana Program as of July 2023 (CCC Industry Report, Feb 2025); largest age group 36–45; mental and behavioural disorders comprised 53% of diagnoses. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2025/02/cannabis-control-commission-releases-industry-report-assessing-adult-and-medical-use-cannabis-markets/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Licensing pipeline (Industry Report): 1,747 licence applications received from inception through April 30, 2024; 650 licensees operating in the study period, 259 of them RMD/MTC-priority establishments; the 700th notice to commence operations issued September 2024. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2025/02/cannabis-control-commission-releases-industry-report-assessing-adult-and-medical-use-cannabis-markets/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

6.3 Saturation and capacity

  • Retailer saturation: 5.1 retailers per 100,000 residents statewide average; highest in Berkshire (18.9) and Hampshire (16.6) counties; lowest in Norfolk (1.4) and Middlesex (3.7) (CCC Industry Report — see Part 3.6 for the RMD/MTC density figures). https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2025/02/cannabis-control-commission-releases-industry-report-assessing-adult-and-medical-use-cannabis-markets/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.
  • Canopy capacity: 4.57 million sq ft of Cultivator + Microbusiness canopy as of December 2025 (CCC, Jan 2026); the CCC began tier-relegation reviews at cultivator renewals starting January 2026. The cultivator-application moratorium to June 30, 2027 (Part 4.4) is the CCC's stated market-stability response. https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/01/massachusetts-adult-use-cannabis-sales-generated-1-65-billion-in-2025/ — Accessed 2026-10-23.

6.4 Prices (official CCC price monitoring)

From the CCC's per-gram CSV series (a_sales_au_price_per_gram.csv, CCCLastUpdated 2026-10-20 — Part 5.2), average retail price per gram: Nov 2018 $14.17; peak ≈$14.68 (Jan 2020); Dec 2024 $4.45; Dec 2025 $4.01; Apr 2026 $3.89; Sep 2026 $3.78 (latest month). Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/resource/a_sales_au_price_per_gram.csv — Accessed 2026-10-23. The CCC Industry Report (Feb 2025) gives a separate series — price per gram fell from $14.09 (Nov 2018) to $5.36 (Apr 2024), −62%, and price per ounce from $401.50 (2018) to $153.73 (2024), −62% — and attributes expected decreases to market saturation, warning that neighbouring-state legalisation may reduce cross-border purchases. Source: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2025/02/cannabis-control-commission-releases-industry-report-assessing-adult-and-medical-use-cannabis-markets/ — Accessed 2026-10-23. The two November 2018 starting figures belong to different CCC series: cite the CSV series ($14.17) for the monthly series and the Industry Report series ($14.09) only when quoting the Industry Report — label each series by source.

6.5 Banks and credit unions

No official CCC or state list of cannabis-friendly banks/credit unions was found — Unavailable from official sources. Secondary (non-official) reporting over several years has named Massachusetts institutions serving the industry — including GFA Federal Credit Union (Gardner; via its subsidiary Lighthouse Biz Solutions), BayCoast Bank (Swansea), Needham Bank, Avidia Bank, BankFive, Dedham Savings Bank and Main Street Bank — but none of these was verified from the institution's own current official page in this run, so they are recorded here as Verify, not as confirmed cannabis-friendly as of October 2026.

6.6 Industry events in Massachusetts

Massachusetts Cannabis and Hemp — Corrected Parts 7–9

Date accessed for all sources: 2026-10-23. Consistency baseline: Run 1 Parts 0–6 (both adult-use 21+ and medical legal; Chapter 65 of the Acts of 2026; taxes 10.75% excise + 6.25% sales + up to 3% local, max 20%, medical exempt, 280E decoupling St.2022 c.180; municipal not county rules, 351 cities/towns, HCA fee ≤3% ending after 8th year; edibles 5 mg/100 mg nominal, 5.5 mg/110 mg with variance, 935 CMR 500.150 eff. 2024-11-22; daily limit 2 oz = 10 g concentrate THC / 1,000 mg edible THC, CCC Admin Order No.6, 19 Apr 2026; beverages single-serving ≤5 mg Δ9-THC; medical qualifying conditions named list plus provider discretion, no patient fee, 60-day supply; hemp MDAR sole authority c.128 §§116–123, 330 CMR 32.00; Metrc seed-to-sale; 935 CMR 500 Ch.65 Emergency Regs eff. 2026-06-18, 501 eff. 2026-01-02, 502 repealed/incorporated, 105 CMR 725 no longer in effect; CY2025 adult-use sales >$1.65B, cumulative crossed $10B 5 Sep 2026, 782 operating businesses, 91 medical dispensaries Sep 2026, cultivator moratorium to 30 June 2027; federal 91 FR 22714 eff. 28 Apr 2026 Schedule III for FDA-approved/state-licensed medical, adult-use Schedule I; PL 119-37 §781 total-THC hemp + 0.4 mg/container eff. 12 Nov 2026; Question 8 proposal NOT law).

Flag key: Verified live = official page opened and read directly in Run 2. Index = search-index extract only. Verify = uncertain/conflicting/unavailable — do not rely without follow-up. Unverified = not confirmed by any primary source.

Summary

  • Part 7: 330 CMR 32.03(1) creates only two licence kinds — a License for Production and a License for Research (a person may not hold both in the same period). Producer / Processor / Dual are MDAR programme application types; processing is governed by c.128 §118 and the Hemp Processor Policy, not by a 330 CMR 32 licence type. MDAR lists three commercial programme application types — Producer (Grower), Processor, Dual. Fees $100 application + $300 / $300 / $500. Research fee amount is unpublished in 330 CMR 32.00 and on MDAR's licensing pages — the regulation provides only for a non-refundable application fee plus a licence fee "established by the Department". Confirm with MDAR Hemp Program, mahemp@mass.gov / 508-281-6783. New applications December 1 – April 30 per MDAR page (regulation-text January 1 reading stays Verify, draft-headed copy). Renewal 1 Oct–1 Dec, expiry 31 Dec, non-transferable, FBI Identity History Summary (within 60 days) for Producer/Dual on the MDAR page, and Research License applicants must also provide an Identity History Summary for all Key Participants overseeing the Research. MA runs its own USDA-approved State Plan (approved 7 May 2020, updated 20 Dec 2021); MDAR is the sole licensing entity. The list is published as "Massachusetts Hemp Licensee List" at https://www.mass.gov/info-details/massachusetts-hemp-licensee-list (all active licences under c.128 §118 and 7 CFR 990; site information withheld). Under c.128 §118(f), only the address of a licensee's cultivation/production facilities, security schematics, and GPS coordinates are confidential; all other application documents ARE public records under c.66.
  • Part 8: No Part 8 listing is confirmed by any primary source. Listing existence, price, status and zoning are all Unverified (broker sources only). Nothing in Part 8 should be described as "verified" — at most "a listing page was opened on 2026-10-23". Zoning is unstated in every case and must be confirmed with the host municipality. The listings covered are: North Adams lease; a Tier 1 Lowell-area business+real estate at $1,500,000; a Boston retail licence at $100,000; Westfield $5,800,000; Winchendon $3,600,000 with conflicting tier descriptions — all Unverified. Several apparently-Massachusetts results were stale or, on opening, in another state (Michigan) and are excluded.
  • Part 9: CCC is the cannabis regulator; MDAR hemp + pesticides; DOR marijuana taxes (Form MRT, cannabis line 617-887-6665); DPH retains only residual roles (health/safety standards consultation under c.94G §4, food-establishment jurisdiction 105 CMR 590, historical pre-2017 medical programme and testing protocol); ABCC has no cannabis licensing role, but it does exercise a direct hemp-related enforcement role over its own licensees under its 30 May 2024 Advisory. CCC phones: Public information line (mass.gov org page): (833) 869-6820, M–F 10am–3pm. Commission's own website and press releases: (774) 415-0200. List both with their channel; neither is an error. Official ballot results: 2008 Q2 Yes 65.2%, 2012 Q3 Yes 63.3%, 2016 Q4 Yes 53.7% (PD43+, share of Yes+No votes). DPH's official RMD status document records Alternative Therapies Group (Salem) as "Approved to Sell" on 23 June 2015 — the first such approval; the 24 June 2015 first-sales/opening date rests on contemporaneous secondary reporting only and remains Unverified. First adult-use retail sales began 20 November 2018 (CCC). UMass Amherst/WCVB Oct 5–12 2026 poll (N=800, official UMass release): Question 8 repeal — 61% No / 31% Yes.

Findings

Part 7 — Hemp Licences

Sources (verified live, accessed 2026-10-23): MDAR licensing https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-industrial-hemp-program-licensing ; renewal https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-hemp-license-renewal ; FAQ https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs ; 330 CMR 32 page https://www.mass.gov/regulations/330-CMR-3200-hemp-production (regulation date shown 08/05/2022; authority MGL c.128 §§116–123); hemp-to-marijuana-establishments FAQ https://www.mass.gov/guides/faqs-sale-of-hemp-to-licensed-marijuana-establishments ; programme page https://www.mass.gov/industrial-hemp-program . Public programme contact on those pages: MDAR Hemp Program, 225 Turnpike Road, Room 302, Southborough, MA 01772; 508-281-6783; mahemp@mass.gov (official programme contacts only).

330 CMR 32.03(1) creates only two licence kinds — a License for Production and a License for Research (a person may not hold both in the same period). Producer / Processor / Dual are MDAR programme application types; processing is governed by c.128 §118 and the Hemp Processor Policy, not by a 330 CMR 32 licence type. Source: https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download (32.03(1),(5),(6))

Licence / programme type Allows Application fee Licence fee
Producer (Grower) — MDAR programme application type Grow/produce hemp $100 non-refundable $300
Processor — MDAR programme application type Process hemp $100 $300
Dual Producer/Processor — MDAR programme application type Both $100 $500
Research — License for Research under 330 CMR 32.03(1) Research only — see quote Research fee amount is unpublished in 330 CMR 32.00 and on MDAR's licensing pages — the regulation provides only for a non-refundable application fee plus a licence fee "established by the Department". Confirm with MDAR Hemp Program, mahemp@mass.gov / 508-281-6783. Verify Verify — see application-fee note

Fees source: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-industrial-hemp-program-licensing ; https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs . Research fee source: https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download (32.03(2)(d)–(e): fee "established by the Department", no amount).

  • 330 CMR 32.03(1) (text read in a stage.mass.gov copy headed <> — wording Verify against the official PDF): "No Person shall Produce Hemp or use Hemp Stock for Production or Research without first obtaining a License from the Department. (a) A Person may apply for either a License for Production or a License for Research. No Person may apply for both a Production and Research License during the same licensing period. (b) All Licenses are nontransferable. (c) Hemp cultivated for Research shall not result in the Production of Hemp or Hemp-derived products." Source: https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download
  • New applications: MDAR page states December 1 – April 30, mailed to the Southborough address above. Source: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-industrial-hemp-program-licensing — this is the operative instruction to applicants. Conflict — Verify: the 330 CMR 32.03(2)(c) text read says new applications "between January 1st and April 30th". The Jan 1 date was read only in the mass.gov-hosted copy headed <>. Confirm in the official PDF/print of 330 CMR 32.00 via https://www.mass.gov/regulations/330-CMR-3200-hemp-production (Downloads) or Secretary of the Commonwealth, Regulations Division. Until then cite the MDAR page window (Dec 1) for applicants and flag the regulation-text conflict. Source for regulation text: https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download (32.03(2)(c))
  • Application steps: review 330 CMR 32.00 and the 2024 Hemp Processor Policy; review the Policy Statement on Sale of Hemp-Derived Products and DPH CBD/THC-in-food policy; review the USDA Final Rule; provide a map for each growing/processing site (site name, address/parcel, GPS in decimal degrees, marked boundaries). Sources: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-industrial-hemp-program-licensing (steps 1–3, step 5); 330 CMR 32.03(5)–(6) via https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download
  • FBI check: Producer or Dual applicants must provide an FBI Identity History Summary Report for each Individual Applicant/Key Participant, current to within 60 days of submission. Processor-only applicants are NOT required to submit an FBI report. Source: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-industrial-hemp-program-licensing (step 6). Research License applicants must also provide an Identity History Summary for all Key Participants overseeing the Research, dated within 60 days prior to submission (330 CMR 32.03(6)). Production License applications require the same for all Key Participants (32.03(5)). The MDAR-page FBI exemption is for Processor-only applicants. Source: https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download (32.03(6) item 10; 32.03(5) item 8). Renewal FBI: new Key Participants within 60 days; reports kept on file 3 years; new/updated every 3 years. Source: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-hemp-license-renewal (step 5)
  • Processor/Dual: proof of permits/approvals from the municipal fire safety official AND municipal building official (fire, safety, building, zoning compliance). Processing must be in areas zoned for such use; State Building Code, Comprehensive Fire Safety Code, 527 CMR 1.00 cited. Source: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-industrial-hemp-program-licensing (step 7)
  • Renewal: October 1 – December 1 annually; $100 renewal fee plus the same licence fees; licences expire annually December 31; all licensed hemp activities must cease if not renewed. Licences are NOT TRANSFERRABLE — new applicants and name/business-name changes make a licence ineligible for renewal (new application required); changes to growing/processing locations do NOT require a new licence. Sources: https://www.mass.gov/how-to/ma-hemp-license-renewal ; 330 CMR 32.03(1)(b) "All Licenses are nontransferable" via https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download . Renewals not received by December 1 may still be submitted until April 30 of the following year but may be assessed an additional late fee (32.03(3)(c)); renewal requires the end-of-year report (32.03(3)(b)), due December 15 (32.04(4)(d)). Source: https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download (32.03(3)(b)–(c); 32.04(4)(d)). Caveat: text read in the mass.gov-hosted copy headed <> — verify pagination against the official PDF before quoting in litigation.
  • State-run vs USDA — FAQ verbatim: "Yes. The MA Hemp Program is currently operating under a State Plan approved by USDA on May 7, 2020 and updated on December 20, 2021." And: "No. The MA Hemp Program is the sole licensing entity for hemp production in Massachusetts. You must be licensed by MDAR if you intend to engage in the production or research of hemp in Massachusetts." Consistent with Run 1. Source: https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs
  • MDAR is sole hemp authority under c.128 §§116–123, 330 CMR 32.00. Sources: https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs ; https://www.mass.gov/regulations/330-CMR-3200-hemp-production . 330 CMR 32.00 regulation date 08/05/2022; regulatory authority MGL c.128 §§116–123. Source: https://www.mass.gov/regulations/330-CMR-3200-hemp-production
  • Hemp definition: Current c.128 §116 text: hemp = genus cannabis with Δ9-THC ≤0.3% dry weight "or the combined per cent of delta-9-tetrahydrocannabinol and tetrahydrocannabinolic acid in any part of the plant … regardless of moisture content." Source: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXIX/Chapter128/Section116
  • Total THC = delta-9 THC + (THCa × 0.877); testing by HPLC. Sources: https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs ; 330 CMR 32.02 "Total THC" definition via https://stage.mass.gov/doc/330-cmr-3200-draft-hemp-production/download
  • Registry/list: FAQ verbatim: "The list of MA Hemp Growers and Processors contains information for all active hemp licenses issued in the Commonwealth pursuant to M.G.L. c. 128, Section 118 and 7 CFR 990. Hemp growing and processing site information is confidential and not subject to public disclosure as set forth in M.G.L. c. 128, Section 118." Source: https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs . The list is published as "Massachusetts Hemp Licensee List" at https://www.mass.gov/info-details/massachusetts-hemp-licensee-list (all active licences under c.128 §118 and 7 CFR 990; site information withheld). Under c.128 §118(f), only the address of a licensee's cultivation/production facilities, security schematics, and GPS coordinates are confidential; all other application documents ARE public records under c.66. MDAR's list page/FAQ describe "site information" as confidential in those terms. Sources: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXIX/Chapter128/Section118 (§118(f)); https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs ; https://www.mass.gov/info-details/massachusetts-hemp-licensee-list . Name + official link only for individuals named on the list; no personal phone, email, mailing address, site address or GPS.
  • MDAR is sole hemp authority under c.128; CCC has no Hemp Program role except coordination. An MDAR licensee may sell hemp to a CCC-licensed establishment (c.94G §12): raw/unprocessed hemp incl. flower, unfinished material, finished products incl. packaged ready-for-sale flower. Sources: https://www.mass.gov/guides/faqs-sale-of-hemp-to-licensed-marijuana-establishments ; https://www.mass.gov/guidance/sale-of-hemp-and-hemp-derived-products-to-marijuana-establishments . Note the FAQ's reference to the "2021 Processor Policy" is stale naming — current policy is the 2024 Hemp Processor Policy, updated 3 Apr 2024 per MDAR policy page.
  • Delta-8: MDAR verbatim — hemp-derived delta-8 THC products "do not allow … to be processed or sold in Massachusetts"; synthetically derived, remains a controlled substance. Sources: https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs ; https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth
  • CBD in food barred: any food product containing CBD cannot be wholesaled; CBD/THC may not be added to food (DPH policy). Sources: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth ; https://www.mass.gov/doc/mdar-policy-statement-sale-of-hemp-derived-products/download . Hulled hemp seed / hemp seed protein / hemp seed oil as GRAS ingredients are the distinct exception in MDAR/DPH guidance — do not conflate with CBD.
  • Raw flower / whole-plant retail barred; unprocessed/raw plant material for consumer end use cannot be wholesaled, except to a CCC licensee. Source: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth
  • Hemp THC drinks: It is unlawful to manufacture or sell food or beverages containing hemp-derived CBD and/or THC (alcoholic and non-alcoholic) in general retail in Massachusetts (DPH/MDAR joint notice, 29 May 2024; ABCC Advisory, 30 May 2024). Only CCC-licensed establishments may sell THC beverages. The ABCC advisory does not apply to CCC-regulated marijuana products. Sources: ABCC Advisory 30 May 2024 + DPH/MDAR joint notice 29 May 2024, via https://www.WORCESTERMA.GOV/inspections/document-center/abcc-advisory-hemp-derived-cbd-thc.pdf ; DPH CBD/THC-in-food guidance via https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth
  • MDAR regulates the wholesale market only; no retail licence is required or issued to sell hemp-derived products. Source: https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth
