DPH (Dept. of Public Health) Residual roles only: CCC must set health/safety standards "in consultation with the department of public health and the department of agricultural resources" (c.94G §4); DPH/local boards of health have jurisdiction over sale of non-infused food and drink at social consumption establishments under 105 CMR 590.000 (935 CMR 500.141(3)(d)2, verified live in the CCC regulation text; DPH food role corroborated by DPH CBD/THC-in-food guidance, https://www.mass.gov/info-details/faqs-sale-of-hemp-derived-products-in-the-commonwealth ); DPH ran the medical programme and the original testing protocol before the Ch.55 (2017) transfer to CCC (historical; CCC 2018 release confirms DPH previously managed RMD testing and "DPH has overseen the Medical Use of Marijuana Program since its launch in 2014", https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2018/12/administration-of-massachusetts-medical-marijuana-program-to-transfer-to-cannabis-control-commission/ ). Administration and oversight transferred on 23 December 2018 (see 9.2). https://www.mass.gov/orgs/department-of-public-health
ABCC (Alcoholic Beverages Control Commission) Alcohol only (c.138) for licensing. ABCC has no cannabis licensing role, but it does exercise a direct hemp-related enforcement role over its own licensees: its 30 May 2024 Advisory makes it unlawful to manufacture/sell or possess on licensed premises food or beverages containing hemp-derived CBD and/or THC, on pain of suspension/revocation, and directs wholesaler retrieval. ABCC is also the contact/enforcement channel for that ban on alcohol-licensed premises. Source: https://www.WORCESTERMA.GOV/inspections/document-center/abcc-advisory-hemp-derived-cbd-thc.pdf (ABCC Advisory, 30 May 2024). Relevant also because cannabis premises may not sell alcohol/tobacco (935 CMR 500.140(3)(f), 500.141(4)(b)) and hospitality social-consumption spaces shared with alcohol service need a physical barrier / non-simultaneous service (500.141(4)(c)) mass.gov ABCC pages: https://www.mass.gov/orgs/alcoholic-beverages-control-commission — 95 Fourth Street, Suite 3, Chelsea, MA 02150-2358; (617) 727-3040, M–F 9am–5pm (verified via mass.gov ABCC pages, index). Source: https://www.mass.gov/orgs/alcoholic-beverages-control-commission
DOER (Dept. of Energy Resources) Energy standards origin: DOER recommended the cultivation lighting limits adopted by CCC (36 W/sq ft; see Part 11.6); CCC energy rules were developed in consultation with the EOEEA working group under St.2017 c.55 §78(b) (935 CMR 500.120(11), verified live). A standalone current DOER cannabis guidance page was not opened — Verify. Confirm any DOER cannabis-specific guidance via https://www.mass.gov/orgs/department-of-energy-resources or EOEEA; the operative limits themselves live in 935 CMR 500.120 (Part 11), not in a DOER page. https://www.mass.gov/orgs/department-of-energy-resources

Rules level note: Massachusetts cannabis/hemp control in Parts 7–9 is state + municipal (not county). Sources: CCC Guidance for Municipalities, https://masscannabiscontrol.com ; MDAR FAQ on municipal zoning for hemp, https://www.mass.gov/guides/hemp-in-massachusetts-faqs . Hemp nuance (MDAR FAQ): commercial agriculture incl. hemp has c.40A §3 protection from unreasonable local regulation, assessed case-by-case — marijuana is expressly excluded from that protection.

Part 10 — Tribal and Interstate

  • Federally recognised tribes in MA = 2: Mashpee Wampanoag Tribe (federal acknowledgment final determination 2007 — BIA, index: https://www.bia.gov/as-ia/opa/online-press-release/cason-issues-positive-final-determination-acknowledge-mashpee ) and Wampanoag Tribe of Gay Head (Aquinnah) (recognised 1987 — background). Both names appear in the Federal Register Indian Entities list, 89 FR 944 (2024-00109): https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/01/08/2024-00109/indian-entities-recognized-by-and-eligible-to-receive-services-from-the-united-states-bureau-of ; BIA tribal entities list: https://www.bia.gov/bia/ois/dhs/icwa/locate-tribe . Tribal government addresses seen on a municipal page (index): Mashpee — 483 Great Neck Road South, Mashpee, MA 02649, mashpeewampanoagtribe-nsn.gov; Aquinnah — 20 Black Brook Rd, Aquinnah, MA 02535, wampanoagtribe-nsn.gov.
  • Tribal cannabis/hemp operations: "No tribal-government-operated cannabis or hemp enterprise, tribal cannabis code, or tribal–state cannabis compact was found in official sources searched (BIA, CCC, malegislature.gov, official tribal sites) as of 2026-10-23." The Mashpee compact located is a gaming compact (IGRA), not cannabis. The CCC licensee framework is c.94G / 935 CMR 500 only: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section12 . The only cannabis operation found in secondary sources is Tribal Fire, a delivery business described in trade press as owned by an individual Mashpee Wampanoag tribal member and licensed through the CCC like any other licensee (opened Aug 23, 2023; partnered with Theory Wellness / later Suncrafted) — Unverified / secondary-only. Ownership, owner name, and opening date are not confirmed by a primary source. Confirm via the CCC Open Data licence record for the business name: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/open-data/data-catalog/ . Conclusion on the official sources searched: any tribal member in MA participates in cannabis/hemp only as an individual CCC/MDAR licensee; no sovereign tribal programme was found in official sources searched.
  • Interstate commerce — official statute text (verified live): c.94G §12 https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section12 — §12(f) verbatim: "No marijuana establishment shall cultivate, manufacture, sell or otherwise transact business with any products containing cannabinoids other than those that were produced, distributed and taxed in compliance with this chapter or any hemp and hemp products cultivated and manufactured in compliance with a license from the department of agricultural resources pursuant to chapter 128 and is in compliance with regulations set forth by the United States Department of Agriculture."
  • c.94G §13 https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section13 — §13(e) as amended by 2026, 65, §32 eff. April 19, 2026 begins "Notwithstanding chapter 94C of the General Laws and until the import or export of marijuana to or from the commonwealth is not prohibited by federal law…" (possession outside residence >2 oz but ≤3 oz = civil penalty ≤$100 + forfeiture).
  • Interstate trigger / commercial trade: "No dormant/trigger statute authorising commercial interstate commerce and no interstate compact/agreement was found in official sources. The only trigger is personal-use c.94G §7(b), which is inoperative while federal law prohibits import/export. Commercial import/export remains barred in practice by §12(f), §13(e)'s federal-law condition, and 935 CMR 500.105(13)(a)13 (routes must remain within the Commonwealth)." — c.94G §7 https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section7 ; §7(b) is broader than a personal-amounts summary: if import/export "is not prohibited by federal law," a person 21+ shall not be penalised for "possessing, using, purchasing, cultivating, processing or manufacturing any amount of marijuana or marijuana products for personal use."
  • Transport must stay in-state: 935 CMR 500.105(13)(a) — "A Marijuana Establishment or a Marijuana Transporter… shall ensure that all transport routes remain within the Commonwealth." Current text at §500.105(13)(a)13: "ensure that all transport routes remain within the Commonwealth" and routes are randomized (§500.105(13)(a)12) — https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ . Consumers likewise may not travel out of state with marijuana (CCC guidance: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ — "Don't cross state lines with marijuana… also illegal to transport it on a plane, train, boat…"; CCC guidance, consistent with federal prohibition; not itself a c.94G prohibition section).

Part 11 — Operations Rules

Primary source for this whole part (verified live, accessed 2026-10-23): 935 CMR 500.000, Ch.65 Emergency Regulations, eff. 2026-06-18 — https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ . CCC approved emergency amendments 17 June 2026 to meet Ch.65's 60-day deadline (Ch.65 signed 19 Apr 2026); the posted document is titled/effective 2026-06-18. Purpose §500.001 verbatim: "…to implement M.G.L. c. 6, § 223, M.G.L. c. 94G, M.G.L. c. 94I, St. 2012, c. 369, St. 2016, c. 334, St. 2017, c. 55, St. 2022, c. 180, and St. 2026, c. 65…"

11.1 Testing / ITLs (§500.160, read in full)

  • §500.160(1): "No Marijuana Product, including Marijuana, may be sold or otherwise marketed for adult use that is not capable of being tested by Independent Testing Laboratories…" Testing must follow the protocol established under c.94G §15 (CCC Protocol for Sampling and Analysis of Finished Marijuana and Marijuana Products); environmental media (soil, growing media, water) under the Commission's environmental-media protocol.
  • §500.160(2): Marijuana is tested for the Cannabinoid Profile and for contaminants as specified by the Commission including, but not limited to, mold, mildew, heavy metals, plant growth regulators, and the presence of Pesticides. Final ready-to-sell vaporizer products must additionally be screened for heavy metals and Vitamin E Acetate (VEA).
  • §500.160(4): written policy required for above-limit results, including "notifying the Commission within 72 hours of any laboratory testing results indicating that the contamination cannot be remediated and disposing of the Production Batch"; notification must come from both the establishment and the ITL, separately and directly, with a plan for destruction and source assessment. (Matches c.94G §15(a)(3)'s 72-hour ITL reporting duty.)
  • §500.160(5): test results kept ≥1 year; results valid 1 year; expired product may not be sold/transferred until retested. (6) Seeds exempt. (7) Clones subject to testing but exempt from metals. (8)–(10) Lab transport/storage/excess disposal under §500.105(13)/(11)/(12).
  • §500.160(11): "No Marijuana Product shall be sold or otherwise marketed for adult use that has not first been tested by an Independent Testing Laboratory and deemed to comply with the standards required under 935 CMR 500.160." (12) Potency variance for single servings ≤ +/− 10%.
  • §500.160(13) failed contaminant test — licensee shall reanalyze without remediation, remediate, or dispose: (a) reanalysis at the original ITL; if it passes, a sample from the same batch goes to a second ITL for a Second Confirmatory Test; failing that, the product may not be sold without remediation, otherwise dispose. (b) Remediation: new sample, full-panel test at a licensed ITL; maximum two remediation attempts; failure after the second = dispose. (c) Disposal per §500.105(12).
  • Recalls/potency audit: CCC release 13 Aug 2026 (verified live) https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/08/massachusetts-cannabis-control-commission-releases-potency-audit-results/ — "The state's 10 Independent Testing Laboratories (ITLs)"; 10 ITLs (10 operating ITLs) is the CCC's own figure for operating ITLs in its 13 Aug 2026 release — use 10 (CCC, Aug 2026). Label-accuracy range 75%–125% of labelled potency; audit of 63 flower products from 62 producers, 13 rated "red"; red-notice producers must recall & repurpose (new testing), recall & relabel, retest, or destroy; holds placed on noncompliant products; ITLs must upload digital COAs to Metrc after every test; licensees submit one test sample package to a single ITL for all compliance testing.
  • First ITLs: CCC release 7 Nov 2018 (verified live) https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2018/11/independent-testing-labs-authorized-to-start-testing-products-for-the-adult-use-cannabis-industry/ — MCR Labs, LLC and CDX Analytics, LLC were the first two authorised; labs' agents fingerprinted; labs established in Metrc.
  • First adult-use retail sales began 20 November 2018 (Confirmed, CCC): https://masscannabiscontrol.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/20220309_NECANN_Presentation.pdf ("November 20, 2018 — First Adult-use Retail Stores Open"); CCC sales release via https://masscannabiscontrol.com/tag/sales/feed/ ("the first two adult-use stores … opened their doors November 20, 2018").
  • Protocol limit example (CCC Protocol, via CCC enforcement documents, index): Total Yeast & Mold 10,000 CFU/g for processed/unprocessed material; 1,000 CFU/g for CO2/solvent extracts. Unverified until the CCC Protocol for Sampling and Analysis is opened directly.
  • ITL named list = Verify/conflicting. CCC's official route is the Open Data Catalog https://masscannabiscontrol.com/open-data/data-catalog/ (Licences "Commence Ops" CSV), which was not opened. Secondary lists conflict with the CCC operating count: a trade source names Analytics Labs, Assured Testing, CDX, Green Analytics MA, Green Valley Analytics, Kaycha MA, Massbiolytics, SafeTiva (as defendants in MCR Labs' Jan 2025 suit); a CCC Jan 2025 meeting extract names MCR Labs, ProVerde, SafeTiva, Smithers AMS, Indo Laboratories. Do not publish a definitive ITL list without the CCC commence-ops data.

11.2 Licence rules (§§500.005, 500.050, 500.101–500.104, 500.105(10)/(16), 500.360, 500.415, 500.450)

Fees (§500.005) — application / annual: Cultivator Tier 1 $200(I)/$100(O) app, $1,250(I)/$625(O) annual, rising by tier to Tier 11 $2,000(I)/$1,500(O) app, $50,000(I)/$25,000(O) annual (Tiers 4–10 app all $2,000(I)/$1,500(O); annual indoor $20,000→$45,000); Product Manufacturer $1,500/$10,000; Independent Testing Laboratory $1,500/$10,000; Retailer $1,500/$10,000; Social Consumption Supplemental $1,500/$5,000; Hospitality $1,500/$5,000; Event Organizer $1,000/$5,000; Third-party Transporter $1,500/$5,000; Existing Licensee Transporter $1,000/$5,000; Courier $1,500/$5,000; Delivery Operator $1,500/$10,000; Delivery Endorsement $500/$5,000; Microbusiness $0 app, 50% of applicable fees; Research Facility $300/$1,000; Research Permit $1,000/$1,000. Other fees: Change in Name $1,000; Change in Location 50% of licence fee; Change in Building Structure $1,000; Change in Ownership/Control $5,000 per entity / $500 per person; Architectural Review $1,500; Packaging & Labelling Preapproval $50/product; Agent Registration Card $115 application and $115 renewal. Application fees waived for Microbusinesses, SEP/EEA-controlled and certified MBE/WBE/VBE small businesses; 50% annual-fee reductions for SEP/EEA and MBE/WBE/VBE; delivery initial fee 100% reduction for SEP/EEA. Source: 935 CMR 500 §500.005 fee table, https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ . Ownership caps (§500.050(1)(b)): general rule verbatim — "No Person or Entity Having Direct or Indirect Control shall be granted, or hold, more than three licenses in a particular class, except as otherwise specified in 935 CMR 500.000." Retailer exception (§500.050(1)(b)10): no more than six Marijuana Retailer licences (matches Ch.65 c.94G §16). Ch.65 nuance in current text: eligibility for a sixth retailer licence is exclusive to Social Equity Businesses for 12 months from when CCC begins accepting such applications (§500.050(8)(d), St.2026 c.65 §60). Total cultivation canopy cap 100,000 sq ft across all licences incl. Medical Marijuana Establishment licences. ITL/Standards Lab holders may hold no licence in any other class. Delivery: max 3 Delivery Operator + 3 Courier; Social Consumption: max 3 Supplemental + 3 Hospitality + 3 Event Organizer; Craft Cooperative: 1 licence, 100,000 sq ft. Timelines (§500.102): municipality response requested within 60 days (30 days for Social Equity/EEA/SEP applicants; non-response in 30 days = requirement deemed satisfied for those). "The Commission shall grant or deny a provisional license not later than 90 days following notification to the applicant that all required packets are considered complete." Licensure/renewal (§500.103): provisional licence fee must be paid within 90 days of provisional approval or approval expires. "(b) A provisional or final license may not be assigned or transferred without prior Commission approval. (c) A provisional or final license shall be immediately void if the Marijuana Establishment Ceases to Operate or if, without the permission of the Commission, it relocates." ("Ceases to Operate" = closed and not transacting business >60 days with no substantial action to reopen, §500.002.) Licence expires one year after provisional issuance and annually thereafter; "No later than 90 calendar days prior to the expiration date, a Marijuana Establishment shall submit a completed renewal application… as well as the required license fee." Indoor cultivator renewals must include a 12-month energy & water usage report and an updated energy compliance letter (MA Licensed PE or Registered Architect). Changes (§500.104): prior Commission approval + fee for location, ownership (equity holder acquiring/increasing to ≥10% or contributing ≥10% of initial capital), control, structural and name changes; unapproved changes may result in suspension, revocation, or the licence being deemed void. Executive Director may approve location/name changes, new equity owners <10%, reorganisations with no ownership change, court appointees. Insurance (§500.105(10), verbatim): "…general liability insurance coverage for no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence and $2,000,000 in aggregate, annually, and product liability insurance coverage for no less than $1,000,000 per occurrence and $2,000,000 in aggregate, annually… The deductible for each policy shall be no higher than $5,000 per occurrence." If coverage is documented as unobtainable: escrow ≥$250,000, replenished within ten business days of any expenditure — §500.105(10)(a)–(c). Bond (§500.105(16), read verbatim): before commencing operations the establishment must provide "proof of having obtained a surety bond in an amount equal to its licensure fee payable to the Marijuana Regulation Fund" to cover destruction of cannabis goods after a violation, costs/compensation of a Court Appointee, cessation of operations, or other Commission-authorised uses; bonds must be issued by a corporate surety licensed in MA; if a bond cannot be secured, escrow of no less than $5,000 (or Commission-approved amount), replenished within ten business days. Enforcement: §500.360 — administrative fine up to $50,000 per violation (each day/instance/provision may be a separate violation; payable within 30 days; unpaid fines added to renewal fee and licence may not be renewed without payment). §500.415 — licence is void if the establishment ceases to operate, transfers location without approval, or adds a controller or ≥10% equity holder without approval. §500.450 — grounds for suspension/revocation/denial of renewal include: not operational within the projected time; deceptive/false information; failure to comply with c.94G/935 CMR 500 or other applicable law (incl. taxes, child support, workers' comp, insurance); failure to submit/implement a plan of correction (§500.320); assigning/changing ownership without approval under §500.104; violating control limits §500.050(1)(b); lack of responsible operation (incl. unsanitary conditions, agent card misuse, repeated sales to under-21s, repeated failure to verify age). Surrender mechanics beyond cessation/voidness: Unverified — no standalone voluntary-surrender procedure was found in §500.450 in the current text searched ("surrender" appears only for agent cards / surrender as a background-check disclosure item / voluntary surrender of product under administrative hold, §500.321). Confirm via CCC licensing guidance / 935 CMR 500.500 hearings provisions or direct CCC inquiry.

11.3 Cultivation / canopy (§§500.002, 500.050, 500.120)

Canopy tiers (§500.050(1)(c)1, (2)(b)): Tier 1 up to 5,000; T2 5,001–10,000; T3 10,001–20,000; T4 20,001–30,000; T5 30,001–40,000; T6 40,001–50,000; T7 50,001–60,000; T8 60,001–70,000; T9 70,001–80,000; T10 80,001–90,000; T11 90,001–100,000 sq ft of Canopy. Canopy (§500.002) = areas containing flowering/vegetative plants >8 in tall and >8 in wide, each shelving level counted. There is no per-plant count for commercial cultivators — tiers are canopy-based. Tier expansion requires having "sold 85% of its product consistently over the six months preceding the application" (indoor) / harvest season (outdoor); at renewal the Commission may relegate a tier if the licensee "sold less than 70% of what it produced." Outdoor Cultivation = no artificial lighting in the canopy area except to maintain immature/vegetative mother plants. A cultivator may cultivate/process/package and transfer to other establishments, not to Consumers. §500.120 additional rules (read verbatim): all cultivation/processing/packaging in a Limited Access Area, not visible from a public place (§500.120(4)); growing media and plant nutrients must be disclosed (§500.120(2)); only a licensed Cultivator or Microbusiness may cultivate adult-use marijuana for sale (§500.120(3)); soil must meet federal standards incl. ATSDR Environmental Media Evaluation Guidelines for residential soil (§500.120(8)); best practices to limit contamination incl. mold, fungus, pests, mildew (§500.120(9)); plant-nutrient application per St.2012 c.262 and 330 CMR 31.00 (§500.120(10)); "organic" labelling only if consistent with USDA 7 CFR 205 and MDAR pesticide requirements (§500.120(7)); transferors must document testing compliance (§500.120(6)).

11.4 Security / transport (§§500.110, 500.105(13))

§500.110(1) general: positively identify persons (21+); prevent loitering; secure all entrances; Limited Access Areas (§500.110(4): 12"×12" "Do Not Enter" signage, ≥1-inch lettering; visitor badges + escort + log; employee ID badges); finished products in a secure locked safe/vault; perimeter lighting; product not visible from a public place; floor plan and security plan shared with law enforcement; cash-handling procedures. §500.110(3) Buffer Zone: "A Marijuana Establishment Entrance may not be closer than 500 feet from the nearest School Entrance, unless a city or town adopts an ordinance or bylaw that reduces the distance requirement" (straight-line centre-to-centre, or shortest pedestrian path if an impassable barrier intervenes). §500.110(5) enclosed-area security (verbatim in current text): perimeter alarm on all entry/exit points and perimeter windows; failure notification system alerting designated employees within five minutes (§500.110(5) — failure alert within 5 minutes); duress/panic/holdup alarm connected to public safety/law enforcement; video cameras at all entry/exit points, parking lots, safes/vaults, sales areas and all areas where marijuana is cultivated/handled/stored/dispensed or cash kept — angled for clear identification; recordings enabled 24 hours/day, available to the Commission immediately, retained at least 90 calendar days (§500.110(5)a.5: recordings "enabled to record 24 hours each day… retained for at least the preceding 90 calendar days"; motion-activated recording permitted with alerting); clear colour still images; embedded synchronised date/time stamp; operational during power outage for a minimum of four hours; exportable stills (.jpg/.bmp/.gif); back-up alarm system from a different company or approved alternate safeguards; surveillance access limited; all security equipment inspected and tested at intervals not to exceed 30 calendar days; foliage maintained. §500.110(6) outdoor: perimeter fence + Limited Access signage, commercial-grade locks, continuously monitored alarm with 5-minute alert, same camera/90-day rules. §500.110(9)–(10): incidents (diversion, theft, alarm events, etc.) notified to law enforcement/Commission, incident report within 10 calendar days, records ≥1 year; annual security system audit at the establishment's expense, report within 30 calendar days, mitigation plan within 10 business days. Transport §500.105(13) (read verbatim): registered agents only; all product linked to the Seed-to-sale SOR (Metrc); vehicles staffed with a minimum of two agents, at least one always remaining with the vehicle (exception: ITL/testing transport ≤$5,000 wholesale value may use one agent; >$5,000 testing = two, §500.105(13)(a)6); weigh/count + inventory on video before departure and re-weigh/re-count on video within eight hours after arrival; sealed, labelled, tamper/child-resistant packaging; emergency-stop log; full transport manifest kept in the vehicle (origin/destination, product, mileage, times, signatures, vehicle make/model/plate) and retained ≥1 year; routes randomized and remain within the Commonwealth; vehicle video system (storage + driver areas, colour stills, date/time stamp); discrepancies/accidents/diversion reported to Commission + law enforcement within 24 hours; vehicle owned/leased, registered/inspected/insured in MA, Commission-approved alarm, HVAC; product not visible from outside; no external markings indicating marijuana transport; no other products in the same vehicle; no firearms in the vehicle or on a transporting agent (§500.105(13)(c)5: "No firearms may be located within the vehicle or on a Marijuana Establishment [Agent]"); transporting agents must hold a MA driver's licence in good standing and carry their Agent Registration Card (§500.105(13)(g)). Transporters must use best management practices to reduce energy/water usage (§500.105(13)(h)). Storage §500.105(11): adequate lighting/ventilation/temperature/humidity; separate area for outdated/damaged/deteriorated/mislabeled/contaminated/opened product held until destroyed; clean and pest-free; secured per §500.110.

11.5 Waste (§500.105(12), read verbatim)

Exterior waste receptacles locked/secured; liquid waste per MA Clean Waters Act c.21 §§26–53, 314 CMR 3/5/7/12/18 and federal CWA/NPDES; organic/recyclable material per waste bans 310 CMR 19.017; marijuana-containing organic material "shall be ground up and mixed with other organic material… such that the resulting mixture renders any Marijuana unusable for its original purpose", then may be composted/digested at a 310 CMR 16.00-compliant operation; solid waste containing marijuana must likewise be rendered unusable before a permitted transfer/disposal facility. Witness rule — current Ch.65 emergency text (§500.105(12)(d)): "A Marijuana Establishment Agent shall witness and document how the solid waste or organic material containing Marijuana is rendered unusable on-site…" — one agent (singular) shall witness and document rendering unusable; the record includes "the name of the Marijuana Establishment Agent present… with their signature", and the electronic record (date, type, quantity, manner, location, agent name + signature) is kept at least three years (§500.105(12)(b)–(d)).

11.6 Environmental / energy (§§500.105(1)(q), 500.105(15), 500.120(5), 500.120(11))

  • Pesticides §500.120(5): "Application of Pesticides shall be performed in compliance with M.G.L. c. 132B and the regulations promulgated at 333 CMR 2.00 through 14.00. Any testing results indicating noncompliance shall be immediately reported to the Commission, who may refer any such result to the MDAR." (Pesticide regulatory authority remains MDAR — c.94G §4(a½)(xxiii): https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section4 .)
  • Energy §500.120(11): cultivators must meet Commission minimum energy-efficiency/equipment standards and all environmental laws (water quality/quantity, wastewater, solid/hazardous waste, air pollution incl. odor/noise under 310 CMR 7.00) as a condition of final licensure and renewal; adopt best management practices set with the EOEEA working group (St.2017 c.55 §78(b)); report energy/water usage; each renewal includes the prior 12 months' usage. Standards: (a) building envelope (non-greenhouse) per MA Building Code/IECC C402/ASHRAE 90.1 §§5.4–5.5 (existing buildings may show F-1 factory-industrial insulation compliance); (b) lighting — Horticulture Lighting Power Density may not exceed 36 watts per square foot, except for Tier 1 and Tier 2 which may not exceed 50 watts per square foot (§500.120(11)(b)1), OR all lighting on the Design Lights Consortium Horticultural QPL (or approved similar list) with Photosynthetic Photon Efficacy ≥15% above the QPL minimum; non-listed lighting needs a §500.850 waiver + third-party certification; OSHA NRTL/SCC safety certification required regardless; (c) HVAC/dehumidification per Building Code/IECC C403/ASHRAE Ch.6, certified by a MA Licensed Mechanical Engineer; (d) worker safety protocols (e.g., eye protection near horticultural lighting); (e) lighting/HVAC requirements waived if the facility generates ≥80% of total annual on-site energy use from on-site clean/renewable generation (c.25A §§11F, 11F½), with RECs/alternative credits for the remainder purchased and retired annually; (f) energy compliance letter (MA Licensed PE or Registered Architect) before final licensure — Microbusiness/Craft Cooperative Tier 1–2 (or waivered) licensees may use a Certified Energy Auditor/Manager instead.
  • §500.105(15) (all establishments): demonstrate consideration of energy-use reduction opportunities, renewable generation opportunities, electric-demand reduction strategies, and engagement with energy-efficiency programmes under c.25 §21 or municipal lighting plants. §500.105(1)(q): written operating procedures must include energy-efficiency policies (reduction opportunities, renewables, demand reduction, Mass Save-type engagement).

11.7 Sampling, giveaways, discounts, promotions (§§500.105(4), 500.140(12))

  • Display samples §500.105(4)(a)5: "A Marijuana Establishment may display, in secure, locked cases, samples of each product offered for sale… An authorized Marijuana Establishment Agent may remove a sample of Marijuana from the case and provide it to the Consumer for inspection, provided the Consumer may not consume or otherwise use the sample, unless otherwise authorized herein."
  • Prices §500.105(4)(a)6: prices may be posted in-store; a catalogue/price list may be provided to consumers and posted on the website and in store. Employee discounts permitted §500.105(4)(a)9.
  • Audience rule: no TV/radio/internet/social/billboard/print advertising unless ≥85% of the audience is reasonably expected to be 21+.
  • Prohibited §500.105(4)(b)18: price advertising except as permitted in (a)6. §500.105(4)(b)20 verbatim: "Advertising through the marketing of free promotional items including, but not limited to, gifts, giveaways, discounts, points-based reward systems, customer loyalty programs, coupons, and 'free' or 'donated' Marijuana, except as otherwise permitted by 935 CMR 500.105(4)(a)9. and except for the provision of Brand Name take-away bags…" All public advertising must include "Please Consume Responsibly," at least two listed warnings, and the full c.94G §4(a½)(xxvi) FDA warning. Source: §500.105(4), https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ .
  • Ch.65 clarification — CCC Bulletin, 25 Sep 2026 (official): https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/09/bulletin-advertising-compliance-regarding-chapter-65-september-25-2026/ — verbatim: "Chapter 65 amended G.L. c. 94G, § 4 to allow the Commission the option of permitting limited advertising activities within a marijuana establishment, or through an opt-in email list. Chapter 65 did not extend advertising or discounting allowances to Licensee websites, nor did it present any immediate substantial change in compliance policy." Discounts are allowed only in-store or via opt-in email, not on websites. Note: full bulletin text should be re-opened before republication of any longer quote.
  • Vendor Samples §500.140(12): retailers must keep written policies for tracking vendor samples received and provided to employees for quality assessment. Limits at §500.140(12)(d): 1. 4g per strain / ≤7 strains flower; 2. 5g concentrate/extract incl. tinctures; 3. 500 mg edibles (serving ≤5 mg); 4. Five units of sale per Cannabis product line and no more than six individual Cannabis product lines (product line = same SKU). Samples provided to employees are recorded (unique ID reduction, date/time, agent registration number, employee name), entered into the point-of-sale system, and count against the employee's daily purchase limit (§500.140(12)(e)–(f)). Parallel provisions: cultivators §500.120(13), manufacturers §500.130(8).

11.8 Drug testing for licence holders/staff

Negative finding: no CCC-mandated drug-testing regime for licensees or agents was found in the sections of 935 CMR 500 read. Keep as a negative finding ("no mandate found in 935 CMR 500 sections read"), not as proof no testing rule exists anywhere (501 / municipal / employer policies not exhaustively searched). What the regulation requires is paper, not testing: written operating procedures must include "Alcohol, smoke, and drug-free workplace policies" (§500.105(1)(k)) and OSHA workplace-safety policies; an immediate-dismissal policy for agents who divert marijuana, engage in unsafe practices, or are convicted of a distribution-to-a-minor drug offence; agent training of 8 hours annually including ≥4 hours Responsible Vendor Training (Basic Core within 90 days of hire for handling/sale agents) (§500.105(2)). c.94G §2(e) preserves employer authority: the chapter "shall not require an employer to permit or accommodate conduct otherwise allowed by this chapter in the workplace and shall not affect the authority of employers to enact and enforce workplace policies restricting the consumption of marijuana by employees" — https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section2 . Social consumption agents specifically may not consume on shift (§500.141(9)(d), Part 12).

11.9 Research and university licences (§500.147; fees §500.005)

Fees confirmed (Part 11.2): Marijuana Research Facility $300 application / $1,000 annual; Marijuana Research Permit $1,000 / $1,000 — §500.005, https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ . §500.147 (read): human-subject research projects require an Institutional Review Board (IRB) approval (§500.147(4)(a),(d); IRB affiliation with an accredited academic institution, licensed healthcare institution or other licensed research institution is a factor in approval, §500.147(5)(a)7 — this is the university route); research products must be tested under §500.160 before consumption by human or animal subjects (§500.147(2)(f)2); projects with human consumption components must include one or more licensed physicians in good standing to monitor participants (§500.147(2)(g)); animal projects require licensed veterinary doctors (§500.147(2)(i)); non-survey human-consumption participants shall reside in the Commonwealth (§500.147(2)(h)); non-survey research conducted solely within the Commonwealth (§500.147(2)(j)); final reports/findings must be supplied to the Commission and may be public records (§500.147(2)(k)); a facility licensee may operate at more than one approved facility and may co-locate with another establishment if commonly owned and physically separated by a permanent wall with a secure locked entrance (§500.147(3)(a),(b)); possession limited to Commission-approved amounts per permit, separated by permit (§500.147(3)(c),(d)); on-premises consumption only as part of an approved permit project and not if co-located (§500.147(3)(f)); permit applications require investigator CVs, physician/vet names, IRB institution, publication-ready summary, detailed protocol incl. safety, goals, dates, funding attestation, participant information, quantities, the ITL to be used, facility identity, disposal protocol, conflict-of-interest disclosures, and the application fee (§500.147(4)(b)); permits are approved by the Executive Director without a Commission vote (§500.147(5)(b)); a permit "may not give immunity under federal law" (§500.147(5)(c), citing §500.840); denial grounds include no IRB approval, danger to public health/safety, lack of scientific value, unqualified applicant, insufficient protocols/funding — with no administrative hearing entitlement for permit denials (§500.147(6)); Commission may inspect facilities and audit projects (§500.147(7)).

Part 12 — Consumer and Social Rules

  • Paraphernalia / smoke shops. c.94C §32I (official malegislature text, index): https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94C/Section32I — "(a) No person shall sell, possess or purchase with intent to sell, or manufacture with intent to sell drug paraphernalia, knowing, or under circumstances where one reasonably should know, that it will be used to plant, propagate, cultivate, grow, harvest, manufacture, compound, convert, produce, process, prepare, test, analyze, pack, repack, store, contain, conceal, ingest, inhale, or otherwise introduce into the human body a controlled substance in violation of this chapter" — the prohibition is tied to use "in violation of this chapter." Penalty figures stated in secondary/index reading (up to 2 years jail/house of correction and/or $5,000; selling to a person under 18: 3–5 years state prison and/or $1,000–$5,000; premises selling tobacco rolling papers must display the statutory warning ($50–$200); syringe/needle sales under §27 excepted) are index-only — Verify against the statute page before republication. Because lawful marijuana use is not "in violation of this chapter," accessories for lawful use are sold as Marijuana Accessories by licensees (935 CMR 500.002: "Paraphernalia means drug paraphernalia as defined in M.G.L. c. 94C, § 1"); a standalone MA "smoke shop" licence regime was not found — Unverified for any affirmative licensing claim. Retailers may not sell tobacco or nicotine/alcohol-containing marijuana products (§500.140(3)(f),(4)(d),(e)).
  • Driving. c.90 §24 https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXIV/Chapter90/Section24 (verified live) — §24(1)(a)(1) verbatim: "Whoever… operates a motor vehicle with a percentage, by weight, of alcohol in their blood of eight one-hundredths or greater, or while under the influence of intoxicating liquor, or of marijuana, narcotic drugs, depressants or stimulant substances, all as defined in section one of chapter ninety-four C… shall be punished by a fine of not less than five hundred nor more than five thousand dollars or by imprisonment for not more than two and one-half years, or both…" The only numeric per se threshold in the statute is alcohol 0.08; there is NO numeric THC per se limit in c.90 §24 — marijuana OUI is impairment-based. Retailer/patient education must warn that driving under the influence is prohibited by c.90 §24 (§§500.140(6), 501.140(6)).
  • Open container / public consumption (c.94G §13, verified live: https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section13 ): §13(c) public consumption civil penalty ≤$100, except consumption "in a designated area of a marijuana establishment located in a city or town that has voted to allow consumption on the premises where sold"; §13(d) open container in the passenger area of a motor vehicle ≤$500 (trunk/locked glove compartment excluded); §13(a) home-grow visibility/lock violation ≤$300 + forfeiture; §13(b) (as amended by Ch.65) possessing >2 oz within a residence unsecured ≤$100 + forfeiture.
  • Blanket pardon (mass.gov, verified live via index extract): https://www.mass.gov/marijuana-pardons and FAQ https://www.mass.gov/info-details/marijuana-pardons-frequently-asked-questions — verbatim: "Effective April 3, 2024, misdemeanor marijuana possession convictions have been pardoned by Governor Maura Healey"; "The Governor's pardon forgives all Massachusetts state court misdemeanor convictions before March 13, 2024 for what is commonly known as simple possession of marijuana." Exclusions: cases resolved after March 13, 2024; possession with intent/distribution/trafficking/OUI; non-MA incl. federal convictions; CWOFs/non-conviction dispositions; persons under 21 at the time of the pardon. Most people need take no action; a pardon certificate may be requested. Pardon ≠ automatic sealing/expungement.
  • Expungement / sealing (official statute pages, index): c.276 §100E https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartIV/TitleII/Chapter276/Section100E (expungement = "the permanent erasure or destruction of a record"); §100K https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartIV/TitleII/Chapter276/Section100K — a court may order expungement where "the record was created as a result of… (2) an offense at the time of the creation of the record which at the time of expungement is no longer a crime…" (see also §100K¼ for marijuana cultivation/possession/distribution records, per mass.gov law pages, index); expungement petitions/orders are not public records (§100O); courts/commissioner shall expunge within 60 days of an order under §§100F/100G/100H/100K. The §100K¼ pinpoint and the 60-day detail are Unverified until the §§100F–100K¼ texts are opened directly at malegislature.gov. Sealing (§§100A–100D) is distinct from expungement. H.2019 pending study order — not re-verified — Verify at https://malegislature.gov/Bills/194/H2019 .
  • Ballot origins of the rules in this part (Secretary of the Commonwealth, PD43+, percentages are share of Yes+No votes): 2008 Question 2 (decriminalisation) — Yes 1,949,704 (65.2%); No 1,038,523 (34.8%); Blanks 114,768; Total votes cast 3,102,995. 2012 Question 3 (medical) — Yes 1,914,747 (63.3%); No 1,108,904 (36.7%); Blanks 160,545; Total votes cast 3,184,196. 2016 Question 4 (adult-use) — Yes 1,769,328 (53.7%); No 1,528,219 (46.3%); Blanks 81,254; Total votes cast 3,378,801 (53.7% Confirmed). Source: https://electionstats.state.ma.us/ballot_questions/search/year_from:1972/year_to:2018 .
  • Medical programme administration. Administration and oversight of the medical programme transferred from DPH to CCC on 23 December 2018 (Confirmed), as mandated by Ch.55 of 2017 (transfer required by 31 Dec 2018): CCC contemporaneous release https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2018/12/administration-of-massachusetts-medical-marijuana-program-to-transfer-to-cannabis-control-commission/ ; CCC Guidance for Municipalities, https://masscannabiscontrol.com . Medical rules are now in 935 CMR 501.000, eff. 2026-01-02: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-501-000-medical-use-of-marijuana-effective-01-02-26-pdf/ . The colocated regulations, 935 CMR 502.000, were repealed and incorporated into the adult- and medical-use regulations (CCC Know the Laws: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ ); colocation is now 935 CMR 500.003 / 501.003. Do not cite 502 as current.
  • Social consumption (§500.141, read in full in the current emergency text: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ ): three licence types (Supplemental, Hospitality, Event Organizer; fees Part 11.2). Key rules: only marijuana/products purchased on the premises may be consumed there (§500.141(2)(g)–(i), (9)(f)); only accessories provided/sold by the establishment may be used (§500.141(2)(h)); product bought on-site must not leave the premises except in an opaque child-resistant sealed exit bag/container (§500.141(2)(i),(4)(k)) — open beverages/infused liquids must be consumed on-site unless sealed in such a container; point-of-service limit = not more than one-half of the c.94G §7 / §500.140(3)(a) limits at one time (§500.141(3)(b)1 verbatim: "shall not serve to a Consumer at one time more than one-half of the limits established in M.G.L. c. 94G, § 7, 935 CMR 500.140(3)(a), and 935 CMR 500.140(4)(c)"), with an open-bill mechanism and excess placed directly into an exit bag (§500.141(3)(b)2–3); establishments may repackage/portion edibles, beverages and tinctures into single/multiple servings within §500.105(6)/§500.150(4) limits (§500.141(3)(c)); no alcohol or tobacco sale or consumption on the premises (§500.141(4)(b)–(c), (9)(e)); hospitality/event licensees may not share a space with alcohol service absent a physical barrier or non-simultaneous service (§500.141(4)(c)); sales hours default 7:00 A.M.–12:00 A.M. unless the municipality authorises otherwise, sales ceasing ≥30 minutes before the latest authorised time (§500.141(4)(f): "sales and service shall only occur between the hours of 7:00 A.M. and 12:00 A.M., and shall cease no later than 30 minutes prior to the latest time the licensee is [authorised]" unless municipality explicitly authorises otherwise; municipal bylaws control); no sale/service to a visibly intoxicated consumer (§500.141(4)(h),(9)(j)); entry restricted to 21+ with age verification by inspection of proof of identification on entry and at point of sale (§500.141(5)) — current text uses ID inspection at entry and point of sale (§500.141(5)(b)–(c)); consumer information card/signage incl. impairment-onset content and the edibles warning that effects "may be impacted or delayed up to two hours or more" (§500.141(4)(a)3); access to Certificates of Analysis via Automatic Identification Technology, which may not collect/store/use consumer personal data (§500.141(4)(a)2); written SOPs must cover overserving prevention, impaired-consumer transportation assistance (reviewed for comment by the municipal chief law enforcement officer), emergency response, and a policy that agents do not consume while at work (§500.141(2)(n)–(p),(y)); waste via locked (fireproof where combustion is used) receptacles emptied by end of day (§500.141(7)); incident reporting incl. any consumer later arrested for marijuana OUI of which the establishment has actual knowledge (§500.141(8)); indoor smoking areas require negative air pressure, ≥20 complete air changes/hour, MERV 14+ filtration, odor control, a separate 6-ACH system for the rest of the premises, annual certification by a MA Licensed Mechanical Engineer, PPE and smoke-free viewing options for agents, and immediate vacation/closure on ventilation failure with no smoking consumption for ≥48 hours (§500.141(10)(d)–(j) — current text expressly permits an Indoor Smoking Consumption Area meeting these requirements); outdoor smoking areas must be open to the air, fire-code compliant, monitored, separated from non-smoking areas and minors (§500.141(11)); non-infused food: DPH/local board of health jurisdiction under 105 CMR 590.000 (§500.141(3)(d)2).
  • Social consumption prohibitions — current §500.141(9)(a)–(j): (a) sell other than as authorised by §500.141; (b) permit sale/consumption in violation of §500.160; (c) agent infusion/combining into food/drink (consumer self-infusion of lawfully purchased product expressly allowed); (d) agent consumption during a work shift; (e) sale/consumption of tobacco or alcohol on premises; (f) consumption of product not purchased from the establishment; (g) organised game/contest involving competitive/cooperative consumption or awarding marijuana as a prize; (h) advertise/market/brand a prohibited practice; (i) consumption outside designated Consumption Areas; (j) sell/serve to an intoxicated Consumer. Source: 935 CMR 500 §500.141(9), https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ .
  • Social consumption exclusivity — §500.050(6)(g) (Confirmed): "Social Consumption Establishment licenses shall be limited on an exclusive basis to businesses controlled by and with majority ownership comprised of Economic Empowerment Priority Applicants or Social Equity Program Participants; or Microbusinesses and Craft Marijuana Cooperatives, for a period of 36 months beginning on the date on which at least one licensee in each Social Consumption Establishment license class (Supplemental, Hospitality, Event Organizer) has received a notice to commence operations," extendable by Commission vote (+24 months, then further only on affirmative findings). The clock has not started until that commence-operations condition is met. Source: 935 CMR 500 §500.050(6)(g), https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-500-000-adult-use-of-marijuana-effective-06-18-26-pdf-2/ .
  • Social consumption availability: CCC (Oct 2026) expects Supplemental licence applications "in the coming months"; municipalities must opt in first, update zoning, execute HCAs. CCC release: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/2026/10/cannabis-control-commission-recognizes-10-years-of-adult-use-cannabis-in-massachusetts/ ("The Commission expects to begin accepting applications for the Supplemental License type in the coming months"); municipal opt-in via c.94G §3(b) / CCC Municipal Zoning Tracker https://masscannabiscontrol.com/municipal-zoning-tracker/ . No social-consumption licence was shown as open for applications in official sources as of Oct 2026.
  • Visitors / tourism. c.94G §7(a) (index, mass.gov) protects "a person 21 years of age or older" with no residency requirement: possessing/using/purchasing "2 ounces or less of marijuana or the equivalent amount of marijuana products as determined by the commission" (as amended by St.2026 c.65 §§29–30, eff. April 19, 2026; §7(a)(1) as amended by 2026 c.65 §29) — https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section7 . Retailers must verify 21+ with government-issued photo ID (§500.140(2)) and may not knowingly sell more than 2 oz (or equivalent) per customer per day (§500.140(3)(a),(4)(c)). Visitors therefore buy adult-use at the same 2 oz limit as residents. CCC Know the Laws: https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ ("Out-of-state visitors can purchase marijuana… 21+… valid government-issued ID… must enjoy your purchase in Massachusetts"). Visitors may not take marijuana out of Massachusetts (federal law; §500.105(13) in-state transport; CCC guidance).
  • Medical reciprocity — resolved per 935 CMR 501.002: Massachusetts does not recognise other states' medical cards (no general reciprocity). 935 CMR 501.002 defines Qualifying Patient as "(a) a Massachusetts Resident or a non-Massachusetts Resident receiving end-of-life or palliative care or cancer treatment in Massachusetts as determined by a Certifying Healthcare Provider, who is 18+…" (and a parallel under-18 clause). The sole non-resident route is registration as a Qualifying Patient under 935 CMR 501.002 if the non-resident is receiving end-of-life or palliative care or cancer treatment in Massachusetts, as determined by a Certifying Healthcare Provider. Source: 935 CMR 501.002, https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-501-000-medical-use-of-marijuana-effective-01-02-26-pdf/ ; CCC Know the Laws https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ . The regulation controls over a consumer summary page; a blanket statement that only MA residents may purchase medical should not be quoted without this 501.002 exception.
  • Medical home grow — resolved: (1) 935 CMR 501.140(3)(c): "A Registered Qualifying Patient may possess up to 12 flowering plants and up to 12 Vegetative plants, excluding Clones and cuttings. If one or more Qualifying Patients collectively require more than this amount at one residence in order to maintain a 60-day supply, then a Hardship Cultivation Registration is required." (2) 935 CMR 501.027(8) mirrors this: a patient is "prohibited from possessing or cultivating more than 12 Flowering plants and 12 Vegetative plants… without a Hardship Cultivation Registration." (3) Hardship Cultivation (§501.027(1)–(7); c.94I §2(d)) — financial hardship / physical incapacity / no MME within reasonable distance (regulation adds lack of access to essential medicine) — allows a limited number sufficient for a 60-day supply, enclosed/locked only. Baseline rule: 12 flowering + 12 vegetative plants without hardship registration; hardship registration is required only to exceed that baseline / where collective 60-day need requires more. Sources: c.94I §2 https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94I/Section2 ; 935 CMR 501 §§501.027, 501.140(3)(c), https://masscannabiscontrol.com/document/935-cmr-501-000-medical-use-of-marijuana-effective-01-02-26-pdf/ ; CCC Know the Laws https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ . Remaining flag: CCC Know the Laws states "The Commission has not yet implemented the Hardship Cultivation Registration program" — if hardship registration is not implemented, how a patient lawfully exceeds 12+12 is Unverified in practice; confirm with CCC Medical Use of Marijuana Program.
  • Landlord/tenant. c.94G §2(d)(1) (official malegislature text, index): https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section2 — the chapter shall not be construed to "prevent a person from prohibiting or otherwise regulating the consumption, display, production, processing, manufacture or sale of marijuana and marijuana accessories on or in property the person owns, occupies or manages, except that a lease agreement shall not prohibit a tenant from consuming marijuana by means other than smoking on or in property in which the tenant resides unless failing to do so would cause the landlord to violate a federal law or regulation." So: landlords may prohibit smoking and cultivation/production; they may not lease-ban non-smoking consumption absent a federal-law conflict (e.g., federally subsidised housing). Government landlords may prohibit possession/consumption in their buildings (§2(d)(2)).
  • Schools. c.94G §2(d)(3) (index): https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section2 — the chapter does not "authorize the possession or consumption of marijuana or marijuana accessories on the grounds of or within a public or private school where children attend classes in preschool programs, kindergarten programs or grades 1 to 12, inclusive, on a school bus, in any youth center, or on the grounds of or within any correctional facility or detoxification facility." Medical cannabis administration in schools: no MA statute/regulation authorising school staff to administer medical marijuana, or exempting school grounds for patients, was found in c.94G, c.94I, 935 CMR 500/501 searched — none found in official sources searched — Unverified. Confirm via DESE / DPH school-health guidance before stating none exists.
  • Gun ownership conflict. Federal: 18 USC §922(g)(3) makes it unlawful for an "unlawful user of or addicted to any controlled substance" to possess firearms/ammunition, and §922(d)(3) bars transfers to such persons; ATF Form 4473 asks the marijuana-use question. The ATF 2011 Open Letter position (a state medical card is "reasonable cause to believe" the holder is an unlawful user) was sourced only via court reproductions / index, not atf.gov — keep Unverified for the ATF-guidance point, and do not state the 2011 letter as current ATF guidance, especially after the 28 Apr 2026 Schedule III change for state-licensed medical marijuana (see Federal section below). MA c.140 interaction was not reviewed — Unverified. Confirm current ATF position at atf.gov (Open Letters / Form 4473 current revision).
  • Pets / veterinary. FDA official Q&A https://www.fda.gov/news-events/public-health-focus/fda-regulation-cannabis-and-cannabis-derived-products-including-cannabidiol-cbd (index extract of the official page): "FDA has not approved cannabis for any use in animals, and the agency cannot ensure the safety or effectiveness of these products. For these reasons, FDA cautions pet-owners against the use of such products and recommends that you talk with your veterinarian about appropriate treatment options for your pet." No FDA-approved, conditionally approved or indexed animal drug contains CBD (FDA Jan 2025 Federal Register notice, via secondary reporting — Verify). No MA-specific veterinary cannabis/CBD rule was found in official MA sources searched — Unverified (MA Board of Registration in Veterinary Medicine not reviewed). Note the research-regime link: animal research under §500.147 requires licensed veterinary monitors and §500.160 testing before animal consumption (Confirmed, Part 11.9).

Federal context relevant to Parts 10–12

  • 91 FR 22714 (Confirmed): final rule/order effective 28 Apr 2026 placing FDA-approved marijuana drug products and marijuana/extracts/Δ9-THC subject to a state medical marijuana licence in Schedule III; new 21 CFR 1308.13(g); permit-only import/export; expedited DEA registration for state medical licensees (21 CFR Part 1301). Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-04-28/html/2026-08176.htm (91 FR 22714–22723, FR Doc. 2026-08176, AG Order No. 6754-2026; "DATES: Effective April 28, 2026"; Summary: applies "to the extent that any of these are included in an FDA-approved drug product or are subject to a state-issued license to manufacture, distribute, and/or dispense marijuana… for medical purposes"); Federal Register page https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2026/04/28/2026-08176/schedules-of-controlled-substances-rescheduling-of-food-and-drug-administration-approved-products . The published title is narrower on its face ("Rescheduling of FDA Approved Products…") — the state-medical-licence category is in the Summary/regulatory text. Keep both categories explicit.
  • Adult-use remains Schedule I (Confirmed): adult-use / non-medical marijuana remains Schedule I after 91 FR 22714; the broader 2024 NPRM (89 FR 44597) is not this final rule. Source: same govinfo text above. Status of the separate broader rescheduling proceeding / any 2026 hearing or pause was not established from a primary Federal Register / DOJ source — Unverified (see Research Notes).
  • 280E consequence: 280E relief covers only the Schedule III medical/FDA categories, not adult-use. Source: 91 FR 22714, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-04-28/html/2026-08176.htm .
  • PL 119-37 §781 (Confirmed): amends 7 USC §1639o hemp definition to total THC (incl. THCA) ≤0.3% dry weight; exclusions incl. non-naturally-producible cannabinoids, synthesised/manufactured-outside-plant cannabinoids, intermediate products >0.3%, final products >0.4 mg combined total per container; effective 365 days after enactment (enacted 12 Nov 2025 → 12 Nov 2026). Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-04-28/html/2026-08176.htm fn.11 ("As of November 12, 2026, this definition is revised to refer to a 'total tetrahydrocannabinols concentration (including tetrahydrocannabinolic acid) of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis'… Public Law 119-37, sec. 781"); CRS (congress.gov) LSB11381 / IF13136 summary of §781. For republication, cite the enrolled PL 119-37 text at govinfo.gov (PLAW-119publ37) alongside CRS.
  • PL 119-103 §2019 — resolved (Confirmed, with corrected mechanics): PL 119-103 is H.R.6500, Continuing Appropriations and Extensions Act, 2027, enacted 2 Sept 2026 (140 Stat. 987). §2019 "SECTION 781 EXTENSION" verbatim: "Until December 11, 2026, the amendments made by section 781 of division B of Public Law 119-37 (7 U.S.C. 1639o note) shall only apply with respect to products described in paragraphs (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I) of section 297A of the Agricultural Marketing Act of 1946 (7 U.S.C. 1639o) (as amended by such section 781)." Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ103/html/PLAW-119publ103.htm . §2019 does not move §781's effective date and does not use the words hemp/cannabis. Until 11 Dec 2026 it limits §781's application to only the two cited paragraphs — (1)(C)(ii)(I) and (1)(C)(iv)(I), i.e. intermediate and final products containing cannabinoids not capable of being naturally produced by Cannabis sativa L. Those exclusions therefore still apply from 12 Nov 2026. All other §781 amendments (total-THC standard, synthesised-outside-plant exclusion in (II), 0.4 mg/container cap in (iv)(III), etc.) are in practical effect held back until 11 Dec 2026. Corrected statement: "PL 119-103 §2019 limits, until December 11, 2026, the application of PL 119-37 §781 to products described in 7 USC §1639o(1)(C)(ii)(I) and (iv)(I) only (non-naturally-producible cannabinoids)." Do not resolve by assertion what the omission of (II)/(III) means for a specific product — that is a statutory-construction question.
  • DOR marijuana FAQ flag (Confirmed as an outdated-source flag): a DOR marijuana FAQ description of marijuana as federally Schedule I without qualification is outdated as to state-licensed medical marijuana after 28 Apr 2026 (91 FR 22714, above). Adult-use remains Schedule I. The DOR FAQ page itself was not re-opened for this flag.

Research Notes — Combined, updated (still unverified only)

Only items that remain unverified are listed below. Each item states where to confirm it.

  1. Tax revenue figures. FY2025/FY2026 totals conflict across CCC releases ($289M FY2025 vs ~$280M; ~$336M FY2026), DOR reports were not opened, and FY2024 remains unverified. Where to confirm: Mass. DOR monthly revenue reports / marijuana tax collections (Form MRT statistics) at mass.gov/dor only — CCC releases report DOR figures second-hand and conflict.
  2. Hardship Cultivation Registration — implementation status in practice. The regulation baseline is in 935 CMR 501.027 / 501.140(3)(c) (Part 12), and CCC Know the Laws states the Hardship Cultivation Registration program has "not yet implemented" wording; how a patient lawfully exceeds the 12 flowering + 12 vegetative baseline in practice remains unverified. Where to confirm: CCC Medical Use of Marijuana Program, via https://masscannabiscontrol.com/know-the-laws/ and the CCC medical programme.
  3. Boston / Cambridge / Somerville / Northampton local fee gaps. Where to confirm: official municipal codes / fee schedules for each city/town only. No county-level substitute exists — MA cannabis local control is municipal (c.94G §3): https://malegislature.gov/Laws/GeneralLaws/PartI/TitleXV/Chapter94G/Section3 .
  4. MDAR hemp licensee list — direct URL/filename. The list exists; site information is confidential under c.128 §118. Where to confirm: MDAR Hemp Program page at mass.gov (licensing page download link) — extract the direct download URL/filename from the live page; confidentiality point at c.128 §118, malegislature.gov.
  5. DOR collections dataset / data.mass.gov datasets. No DOR collections dataset was opened. Where to confirm: CCC Open Data Catalog https://masscannabiscontrol.com/open-data/data-catalog/ and DOR at mass.gov/dor.
  6. Banks / banking. No primary federal/state source established MA-specific cannabis banking. Where to confirm: FDIC / NCUA / MA Division of Banks official guidance, if it exists; otherwise keep Unavailable.
  7. Question 8 home-grow scope. Whether the Question 8 proposal (a proposal, not law; vote 3 Nov 2026, would take effect 1 Jan 2028 if approved) also ends home cultivation remains unverified. Where to confirm: official full petition / proposed-law text via Secretary of the Commonwealth (sec.state.ma.us) sample ballot / full text only; secondary polling coverage cannot resolve this.
  8. Hemp application window conflict. MDAR page Dec 1–Apr 30 vs draft 330 CMR 32.03 Jan 1–Apr 30; a draft-headed copy cannot resolve it. Oct 2026 releases) appear on official CCC/mass.gov material; this is an official-source inconsistency, not a value to silently resolve. Publish both labelled by source. Where to confirm: CCC Communications, via https://www.mass.gov/orgs/cannabis-control-commission and https://masscannabiscontrol.com .
